UK Government Lied Over UFO FOI Figures




                                                          UFO Updates
                                   A mailing list for the study of UFO-related phenomena

                                        UK Government Lied Over UFO FOI Figures
  From: Dave Haith <visions1.nul>
  Date: Mon, 1 Mar 2010 18:17:08 -0000
  Archived: Mon, 01 Mar 2010 19:55:15 -0500
  Subject: UK Government Lied Over UFO FOI Figures

  UFO researcher Andy Russell seems to have caught the
  Government, Nick Pope and Dr David Clarke twisting the
  facts over Freedom of Information enquiries on UFOs.
  At his blog:
  http://thetruthhides.wordpress.com
  Andy has checked out the figures and his sums add up differently
  to those given by the authorities as a reason for closing down
  the MOD's reporting facility.
  It would be interesting to hear Nick and David's reaction
  to this, as I know they are both members of this List.
  In an investigation, headed MoD UFO Desk -- The Real Story
  Andy, from Wales, writes:
  -----
  Why after 50 years has the British Government closed down its
  Ministry of Defence UFO desk?
  Official reasons given by the Government and echoed by Nick Pope
  and Dr David Clarke are that it's too costly and the flood of
  UFO Freedom of Information Act enquiries is taking up an
  increasing and unacceptable amount of time.
  The facts have shown that both reasons are bunk -- and even the
  Under Secretary of State has been caught repeating a blatant lie
  over the figures.
  On 23rd December 2009 the UK Government called time on the UFO
  Desk, the MoDs UFO reporting facility. Despite operating for
  more than 50 years it was now deemed to be a resource the
  British Government could no longer justify. According to The
  Times, the cost per year was around =A344,000. When we put into
  context later how much that equates to in the grander scheme of
  things, we can see that it is a drop in a mighty ocean. What was
  surprising to me was the evidence that the Government, Nick Pope
  and Dr David Clarke have put forward to re-enforce its position
  on this decision.
  When we look at the decision objectively, with some actual data
  and not just vague comments from the aforementioned, we see that
  the UFO Desk is not a resource that is particularly draining on
  the MoD or the British taxpayer.
  First stop in our assessment of the data is the data itself.
  Professor Alasdair Roberts, of Sussex University Law School
  wrote a paper entitled =91Preliminary Analysis of Data Relating to
  the Processing of Freedom of Information Act Requests Received
  by the UK Ministry of Defence (Revised May 2009). Professor
  Roberts took data received from the MoD which showed all the
  FOIA requests made since 2005 and broke it down into categories
  such as the type of people making requests (such an ex-employee,
  media/journalists, private individuals etc), whether the
  requests received the requested documents etc. So in short, it
  was a snapshot of the FOIA function of the MoD.


http://www.ufoupdateslist.com/2010/mar/m01-001.shtml[10/12/2011 14:18:59]
UK Government Lied Over UFO FOI Figures


  Professor Roberts highlights that since 2005 FOIA requests to
  the MoD have been declining. He also explains that the reason
  FOIA requests (in general) were so high is because there was a
  =91pent up' need for information that the FOIA was now addressing
  and since then there was a decline in requests that was expected
  to continue to this present day. And judging by the fact that
  Professor Roberts was updated in May 2009, we can see that this
  assumption is proving correct.
  In relation to UFO requests the decline in requests is also
  apparent.
  If we take a quick snapshot:
  2005: 199 UFO related requests
  2006: 140 UFO related requests
  2007: 120 UFO related requests
  So we can see the diminishing requests for information. Now, out
  of the data of the 15,627 requests made in total to the MoD,
  there are just 459 requests pertaining to UFOs. So our UFO
  requests snapshot equates to only 2.93% of enquiries.
  Which is hardly the picture Nick Pope, Dr Clarke and the
  Government are painting.
  Now, in their official briefings, as highlighted on Dr Clarke's
  blog dated 18 January 2010, the MoD did not expect the volume of
  UFO requests to reduce, but according to the MoDs own data, that
  demand was diminishing every year. Yet these same MoD officials
  were able to effectively lie to the Secretary of State and claim
  that the work regarding responding to UFO FOIA enquiries was
  "[distracting] them from more important priorities".
  As we have looked at the data we have seen that this only
  equates to 2.93% of their FOIA requests. There are larger
  categories of requests such as the Iraq war, the Afghanistan
  conflict, yet the MoD is quibbling about 2.93%? Do not insult
  our intelligence.
  According to Under Secretary for Defence Tom Watson "There is a
  real and enduring interest in Unidentified Flying Objects. By
  far the most popular topic of FOI requests has been UFOs,
  followed by recruitment enquiries, enquiries from staff, and
  historical events such as World War Two, the Falklands conflict
  and the Balkans. Recent freedom of information releases on UFOs
  have attracted media interest from as far away as Japan."
  (http://www.direct.gov.uk/en/Nl1/Newsroom/DG_10036975)
  But this is an out and out lie! And even IF they were the most
  popular topic, they still represented only 2.93% of the overall
  requests made.
  Another good snapshot of the small amount of UFO FOIA requests
  made to the MoD comes via the website whatdotheyknow.com. This
  is an automated service which allows users to auto-generate and
  auto-submit FOIA requests to any branch of Government. The great
  thing about this is that we can see the requests and their
  responses. Now, between 17/05/2008 and 19/02/2010
  whatdotheyknow.com submitted 323 enquiries to the MoD. Of those
  323 enquiries only 3 were relating to UFOs. That calculates as
  0.93% of FOIA relating to UFO submissions via the website. It's
  another snapshot of how small a percentage the UFO requests
  actually are.
  Just to put the boot in further, if we look at the latest FOIA
  Disclosure Log (a log of all the FOIA requests the MoD has
  received between 7th December 2009 and 1st January 2010) the
  amount of UFO related requests is 4.08% of the total requests
  made. Again, a very small amount.
  Now, 2009 was supposed to be a bumper year for reports, but that
  doesn't really match with the claims that the UFO Desk was being
  closed because of FOIA requests. Requests. Not reports.
  Now, Nick Pope claims that in 2007 there were more requests
  about UFOs than anything else:
  http://www.nickpope.net/mod-ufo-files.htm



http://www.ufoupdateslist.com/2010/mar/m01-001.shtml[10/12/2011 14:18:59]
UK Government Lied Over UFO FOI Figures

  yet the MoDs data shows that this is not the case. Only 120
  requests were made. So where Nick has obtained this information
  from is beyond me, as the MoDs actual data tells a different
  story and this is further verified by Professor Roberts' paper.
  According to Nick "hundreds of people were bombarding the MoD
  with requests":
  http://www.nickpope.net/mod-ufo-files.htm
  Er, no, they weren't. That year it was only 120.
  But when Nick was told that all the files were being released
  due to this "administrative burden" (same web page), Nick very
  generously broke the story to the media. Shame you didn't check
  the facts first, Nick.
  So, let us put the gargantuan cost of the UFO Desk into context.
  According to The Times, the cost is =A344,000 and the UFO Desk
  employed one person. (That's right. One. Now employed elsewhere
  in the MoD)
  The MoD entire budget is around =A339.6billion. Whilst the
  Government is looking to cut what it deems unnecessary burdens
  maybe they should have looked at the 141 days lost (man-hours in
  total) as it gave MoD troops and billion-pounds worth of
  equipment to be used in BBC's Top Gear programme. So let me get
  this straight, the Government quibbles over =A344k and security of
  its airspace, yet troops and their equipment can go play with
  Jeremy Clarkson for a grand total of 141 days? (As reported in
  The Guardian). The Government and Nick Pope can wring their
  hands in horror at the "administrative burden" and the costs
  attached to that, but the Government paid over =A337 million in
  bonuses of MoD staff in 2009.
  The Governments of this world, and the "experts", can all come
  forward all give us their sound-bite and their "insights" as to
  why decisions are made, but in this case, as in so many others,
  the data does not support their claims. There is no desk being
  "bombarded" with UFO requests, there is no "administrative
  burden", there is no desk costing the MoD huge amounts of cash.
  We know this because the MoD's own data tells us so.
  What there is, is a group of liars, a group of people who expect
  the general public not to ask questions, not to dig in the
  background, but to blindly accept what they have been told. The
  MoD has been looking to distance itself from the UFO subject for
  a long time, and this is just the flimsy excuse it will use. The
  MoD will get the usual suspects to toe the party line and tell
  us half-truths to further its cause.
  This is a continuation of the MoD's end-game or exit strategy
  regarding UFOs. So in the future, when there is another
  sighting, another incident or another Rendlesham, the MoD can
  smugly say that is has nothing to do with UFOs, it was all
  closed down due to the "administrative burden" and the "hundreds
  of requests" it was receiving, plus all the resources it was
  taking up.
  Or, as the press and others have just reported, the MoD will
  destroy all UFO reports after 30 days. What other Government
  department could get away with such a stance? Your local Police
  authority will now start destroying crime reports after 30 days?
  Your local Social Services will start destroying reports of
  children at risk after 30 days? As with all Government
  departments it's a case of =91smoke and mirrors'.

  Andy Russell

http://www.ufoupdateslist.com/2010/mar/m02-002.shtml[10/12/2011 14:19:00]
Childerhose Photo




                                                          UFO Updates
                                   A mailing list for the study of UFO-related phenomena
                                               'Its All Here In Black & White'
    Location: UFOUpDatesList.Com > 2010 > Mar > Mar 2

                                                             Childerhose Photo
  From: Martin Shough <parcellular.nul>
  Date: Tuesday, March 02, 2010 9:56 AM
  Archived: Tue, 02 Mar 2010 18:41:26 -0500
  Subject: Childerhose Photo

  Hi
  Thanks to V-J for providing chapter and verse on this claim.
  Campbell's evidence for a hoax is:
  1) what he claims to be an "unexplained reversal" of the print
  (left to right) and
  2) a comment by the flight leader Annis about letting Childerhose
  "have his fun".
  As regards 1) the basis of his argument is this: Childerhose
  claimed he was looking out of the left side of his plane,
  therefore, if he was heading west, the photo must be taken
  looking south. If the photo direction is south, and if the sun
  illumination is "from the left", i.e. from the east, it cannot
  have been taken at sunset as reported. Therefore the photo must
  have been laterally reversed
  But there is real doubt that the object was on the "left side of
  the aircraft". Also, although the route of the flight was
  broadly East-West this does not mean that the instant heading of
  the aircraft at the time must have been due west - Childerhose
  recalled some manoeuvering in relation to the storm tops.
  (Neither is it necessarily the case that the fore-aft axis of
  the aircraft would have been exactly aligned on the flight
  heading if there were strong winds requiring a small crabbing
  angle - although at over 400kt this likely to be a small
  factor.)
  The actual azimuth of the setting sun from sea level that day
  was about 285 deg. From 36,000ft the horizon dip angle would
  have been about 3 degs so it would not have set until some 13
  minutes later and significantly further north, about 290 deg. So
  actually the sun would have set in the WNW.
  In any case, if the correct time was 7:20 pm, twenty minutes
  before sea-level sunset, the sun should have been about 11 deg
  above the apparent horizon from 36,000ft (8 deg above the
  sea-level horizon) and only a few degs away from due West. Thus
  Campbell appears to be correct that there is a mismatch with the
  sun direction. IF the object was on the left of the plane. This
  apparent mismatch has of course been discussed elsewhere, e.g. in
  some detail by Bruce Maccabee
  http://www.brumac.8k.com/RJC/RJC.html
  but without clear resolution because Childerhose himself was
  confused. The statement relied on by Campbell - that the object
  was to his left - was apparently made over 10 years after the
  event at a time when Childerhose himself admitted a confused
  memory because of uncertainty about the orientation of the
  aircraft at the time (they possibly turned to avoid a
  thunderhead). So is this reliable? Experience teaches us that we
  would be very wise, on a percentage basis, to cleave to original
  sources.
  Thus Childerhose originally said:



http://www.ufoupdateslist.com/2010/mar/m02-003.shtml[10/12/2011 14:19:00]
Childerhose Photo

  "The sunlight was reflecting on the tops of cumulus formations,
  and was coming from our 10 o'clock position. (This is relative
  to the aircraft's heading.) The white disc was at 1 o'clock."
  In other words, the object was to the _right_ of the nose of
  Childerhose's F-86, and therefore as they flew on it passed down
  the _right_ side of the plane, illuminated by the westering sun
  off to the left of the photo. So this is perfectly consistent,
  internally, and with Childerhose's other photo, reportedly taken
  just "a few minutes" later, which does appear to show the planes
  on a westward heading into the sun.
  I am unclear why one should feel constrained to accept
  Childerhose's later altered version, post-1966, that the object
  was to his left. But, if one wishes to do so, then Campbell's
  proposal, that the print has been flipped left to right, is
  actually quite interesting. The print we are familiar with was
  apparently made by projection from a slide (not, I believe, by
  Childerhose) and it would not be too difficult for this to
  happen. It would explain a great deal. Curiously, therefore,
  this half of Campbell's case for "hoax" is actually a case
  _against_ hoax!
  As regards the other half of Campbell's case, point 2), this is
  an extremely tenuous point that amounts to no more than an
  insinuation based selectively on a third-party character
  assessment. 'Selectively', because although he allows the reader
  to form the impression that Annis' testimony supports the "hoax"
  theory, in fact Annis says that although he didn't see the
  object himself he has always been of the opinion that the light
  phenomenon was a natural refraction effect of some kind. A
  natural light phenomenon is not a hoax. In that context, his
  comment about "letting Childerhose have his fun" evidently means
  that he thought Childerhose was reading too much into it by
  claiming a possible "UFO".
  So I am not very impressed by Campbell's case for a hoax. I'm
  actually rather more troubled by Mike Tarbell's recent argument
  that the nearby clouds appear not to be illuminated as they
  should be. At the least, this probably ought to justify
  considering some revised brackets on distance and thus
  revisiting Bruce's luminosity calculation.
  Martin Shough

                                     Re: UK Government Lied Over UFO FOI Figures
  From: David Clarke <daveclarke292.nul>
  Date: Tue, 2 Mar 2010 17:53:41 +0000
  Archived: Tue, 02 Mar 2010 18:43:41 -0500
  Subject: Re: UK Government Lied Over UFO FOI Figures

  >From: Dave Haith <visions1.nul>
  >To: UFO UpDates <post.nul>
  >Date: Mon, 1 Mar 2010 18:17:08 -0000
  >Subject: UK Government Lied Over UFO FOI Figures
  Dave claimed::
  >UFO researcher Andy Russell seems to have caught the
  >Government, Nick Pope and Dr David Clarke twisting the
  >facts over Freedom of Information enquiries on UFOs.
  Oh no he didn't!
  >Andy has checked out the figures and his sums add up differently
  >to those given by the authorities as a reason for closing down
  >the MOD's reporting facility.
  He may have "checked out the figures" but he used the wrong
  figures. If you intend to make allegations about people twisting
  facts you need to get your own facts right.
  Unfortunately, Andy's claims are based upon the assumption that
  the MoD justified their decision on the basis of a rising number
  of FOI requests. But Andy posted this before he had actually
  read the documents which are now available for scrutiny here:
  http://tinyurl.com/yarsfxc
  As the documents reveal, the justification given is not the cost
  of dealing with FOI requests but the tripling of UFO reports
  from an average of 150-200 per year prior to 2009 to 634 last
  year. The document states quite clearly that "the increased
  workload caused by this upsurge in sighting reports is now
  exceeding the level of staff resource...allocated to this task
  and impacting on other tasks." FOI requests are mentioned only
  in passing,. which totally undermines Andy's case.
  Nevertheless, whatever the justification used, my position has
  clearly been that the closure of the MoD's UFO desk is a short-
  sighted and misjudged decision.
  I was quoted to this effect by Channel 4 news today: "Its
  misguided. The subject will not go away. Sooner or later there
  will be a big sighting by someone like an airline pilot and
  there will be a huge public outcry into why no one is looking
  into [this subject at the MoD]."
  I'm at a loss to understand how this position can be interpreted
  by Andy Russell as me "echoing" the MoD's policy on the subject,
  or "twisting the facts" as you claim.
  In fact exactly the opposite is the case. I am simply reporting
  the facts and publishing the results of an FOI request to the
  wider public.
  Andy has now acknowledged my true position on his website but
  has so far resisted my request for an retraction of his original
  posting.



http://www.ufoupdateslist.com/2010/mar/m02-004.shtml[10/12/2011 14:19:01]

                                                        Secrecy News -- 03/03/10
  From: Steven Aftergood <saftergood.nul>
  Date: Wed, 03 Mar 2010 09:39:16 -0500
  Archived: Thu, 04 Mar 2010 10:27:18 -0500
  Subject: Secrecy News -- 03/03/10

  SECRECY NEWS
  from the FAS Project on Government Secrecy
  Volume 2010, Issue No. 17
  March 3, 2010
  Secrecy News Blog:             http://www.fas.org/blog/secrecy/

  **WHITE HOUSE OFFERS GLIMPSE OF CYBERSECURITY PROGRAM
  **ARMY FORESEES "PERPETUAL TURBULENCE" IN CYBERSPACE
  **USE OF U.S. FORCES ABROAD, 1798-2009, AND MORE FROM CRS

  WHITE HOUSE OFFERS GLIMPSE OF CYBERSECURITY PROGRAM
  The White House yesterday released a newly declassified
  description of the Comprehensive National Cybersecurity
  Initiative (CNCI), a highly classified program that is intended
  to protect U.S. government computer networks against intrusion
  and disruption.
  http://www.fas.org/irp/eprint/cnci.pdf
  The cybersecurity initiative was established in January 2008 by
  President Bush's classified National Security Presidential
  Directive (NSPD) 54, and quickly became controversial in part
  because of the intense secrecy surrounding it.
  "Virtually everything about the initiative is highly
  classified," the Senate Armed Services Committee complained in
  2008, "and most of the information that is not classified is
  categorized as 'For Official Use Only'. These restrictions
  preclude public education, awareness and debate about the policy
  and legal issues, real or imagined, that the initiative poses in
  the areas of privacy and civil liberties.... The Committee
  strongly urges the [Bush] Administration to reconsider the
  necessity and wisdom of the blanket, indiscriminate
  classification levels established for the initiative." No such
  reconsideration was forthcoming until now.
  http://www.fas.org/sgp/congress/2008/sasc-cyber.html
  Concerns about overclassification were also expressed by the
  National Academy of Sciences in a 2009 report, which called for
  "a broad, unclassified national debate and discussion about
  cyber-attack policy," and argued that "secrecy even about broad
  policy issues serves mostly to inhibit necessary discussion
  about them."
  http://www.nap.edu/catalog.php?record_id=12651
  The Comprehensive National Cybersecurity Initiative was "the
  single largest request and the most important initiative of the
  President's fiscal year 2009 [intelligence] budget request," the
  House Intelligence Committee said in its report on the FY2009
  intelligence authorization act.
  http://www.fas.org/irp/congress/2008_rpt/hrpt110-665.html



http://www.ufoupdateslist.com/2010/mar/m04-001.shtml[10/12/2011 14:19:02]
Secrecy News -- 03/03/10

  The Electronic Privacy Information Center filed a Freedom of
  Information Act lawsuit just last month seeking declassification
  and disclosure of the Bush Administration's NSPD 54.
  But that foundational directive was not disclosed, nor did the
  Obama Administration address the issue of offensive cyber policy
  raised by the National Academy. Instead, the White House
  released a descriptive summary of 12 component elements of the
  Cybersecurity Initiative, a gesture that it said was consistent
  with the President's emphasis on increased transparency.
  "Transparency is particularly vital in areas, such as the CNCI,
  where there have been legitimate questions about sensitive
  topics like the role of the intelligence community in
  cybersecurity," said Howard A. Schmidt, the White House
  Cybersecurity Coordinator who announced the disclosure.
  "Transparency provides the American people with the ability to
  partner with government and participate meaningfully in the
  discussion about how we can use the extraordinary resources and
  expertise of the intelligence community with proper oversight
  for the protection of privacy and civil liberties," Mr. Schmidt
  said.
  http://www.fas.org/irp/news/2010/03/wh-cyber.html
  But without a clear delineation of legal authorities and
  implementation mechanisms, the scope for meaningful public
  discussion seems limited.
  As the House Intelligence Committee put it in 2008, "a
  cybersecurity initiative [is] worthwhile in principle, but the
  details of the CNCI remain vague and, thus, open to question."
  In order to bolster independent oversight of programs such as
  the CNCI that must remain classified, at least in part, dozens
  of public interest organizations including the Federation of
  American Scientists this week urged President Obama to finally
  appoint the members of an independent executive branch oversight
  board.
  The Privacy and Civil Liberties Oversight Board, originally
  proposed in 2004 by the 9/11 Commission to monitor and defend
  civil liberties in information sharing and counterterrorism
  activities, was given independent agency status by Congress in
  2007. But it has remained vacant since that time and thus
  unable to fulfill its assigned task.
  "It is crucial that you nominate qualified individuals to serve
  on the PCLOB, so that it may begin to provide guidance as new
  policies and procedures are developed," the public interest
  group letter said.
  http://www.defendingdissent.org/pdf/pclobletter.pdf

  ARMY FORESEES "PERPETUAL TURBULENCE" IN CYBERSPACE
  U.S. Army doctrine published last week anticipates an
  increasingly unstable information environment that may challenge
  Army operations and test national capabilities.
  "Unprecedented levels of adverse activity in and through
  cyberspace threaten the integrity of United States critical
  infrastructure, financial systems, and elements of national
  power. These threats range from unwitting hackers to nation-
  states, each at various levels of competence. Collectively, the
  threats create a condition of perpetual turbulence without
  traditional end states or resolution."
  Under prevailing circumstances, the Army says, "Notions of
  'dominating' cyberspace are simplistic and unrealistic. A
  realistic and meaningful goal is to achieve and maintain freedom
  of action in and through cyberspace while being able to affect
  that of the adversaries."
  The Army's assessment and proposed response are described in
  "Cyberspace Operations Concept Capability Plan 2016-2028,"
  TRADOC Pamphlet 525-7-8, February 22, 2010:
  http://www.fas.org/irp/doddir/army/pam525-7-8.pdf



http://www.ufoupdateslist.com/2010/mar/m04-001.shtml[10/12/2011 14:19:02]
Secrecy News -- 03/03/10


  USE OF U.S. FORCES ABROAD, 1798-2009, AND MORE FROM CRS
  Noteworthy new and updated reports from the Congressional
  Research Service that have not been made readily available to
  the public include the following.
  "Instances of Use of United States Armed Forces Abroad, 1798-2009,"
  January 27, 2010:
  http://www.fas.org/sgp/crs/natsec/RL32170.pdf
  "Afghanistan Casualties: Military Forces and Civilians," February 26,
  2010:
  http://www.fas.org/sgp/crs/natsec/R41084.pdf
  "China-North Korea Relations," January 22, 2010:
  http://www.fas.org/sgp/crs/row/R41043.pdf
  "Honduran Political Crisis, June 2009-January 2010," February 1, 2010:
  http://www.fas.org/sgp/crs/row/R41064.pdf
  "Southwest Border Violence: Issues in Identifying and Measuring Spillover
  Violence," February 16, 2010:
  http://www.fas.org/sgp/crs/homesec/R41075.pdf
  "Satellite Surveillance: Domestic Issues," February 1, 2010:
  http://www.fas.org/sgp/crs/intel/RL34421.pdf

  >aircraft at the time (they possibly turned to avoid a
  >thunderhead). So is this reliable? Experience teaches us that we
  >would be very wise, on a percentage basis, to cleave to original
  >sources.
  >Thus Childerhose originally said:
  >"The sunlight was reflecting on the tops of cumulus formations,
  >and was coming from our 10 o'clock position. (This is relative
  >to the aircraft's heading.) The white disc was at 1 o'clock."
  >In other words, the object was to the _right_ of the nose of
  >Childerhose's F-86, and therefore as they flew on it passed down
  >the _right_ side of the plane, illuminated by the westering sun
  >off to the left of the photo. So this is perfectly consistent,
  >internally, and with Childerhose's other photo, reportedly taken
  >just "a few minutes" later, which does appear to show the planes
  >on a westward heading into the sun.
  >I am unclear why one should feel constrained to accept
  >Childerhose's later altered version, post-1966, that the object
  >was to his left. But, if one wishes to do so, then Campbell's
  >proposal, that the print has been flipped left to right, is
  >actually quite interesting. The print we are familiar with was
  >apparently made by projection from a slide (not, I believe, by
  >Childerhose) and it would not be too difficult for this to
  >happen. It would explain a great deal. Curiously, therefore,


http://www.ufoupdateslist.com/2010/mar/m04-002.shtml[10/12/2011 14:19:03]
Re: Childerhose Photo

  >this half of Campbell's case for "hoax" is actually a case
  >_against_ hoax!
  <snip>
  I used what Childerhose told me was the original slide. James
  McDonald was the first to raise the question of whether or not
  there had been a left-right flip.
  Using the original slide I could determine which side had the
  emulsion and therefore which side (emulsion side) was facing the
  lens. I determined that the photo was not "flipped". The print
  that people 'are familiar with' was, if I recall corresctly,
  made from the original slide by Robert Nathan at JPL...
  There was also a print in Klass' first book, UFOs Identified.

John Burke - The 'B' In 'BLT' Dies




                                                          UFO Updates
                                     A mailing list for the study of UFO-related phenomena
                                                 'Its All Here In Black & White'
    Location: UFOUpDatesList.Com > 2010 > Mar > Mar 5

                                                John Burke - The 'B' In 'BLT' Dies
  From: Dave Haith <visions1.nul>
  Date: Fri, 5 Mar 2010 08:12:59 -0000
  Archived: Fri, 05 Mar 2010 06:39:01 -0500
  Subject: John Burke - The 'B' In 'BLT' Dies
  -----
  From: Nancy Talbot <bltresearch.nul>
  Date: Monday, March 01, 2010 4:16 AM

  Dear Colleagues,
  Our friend and fellow researcher, John A. Burke, has died after
  a struggle with liver disease. John had both the capacity for
  awe and the curiosity of a true scientist and a great deal of
  the work he did was "behind the scenes," partly because he lived
  and worked on both sides of the fence. John recognized that
  there are many unknowns in our world, some of which might never
  be understood in scientific terms, others of which he believed
  could be grasped - and utilized - in a practical world.
  His long effort to bring the "MIR Stressguard" technology - see
  item #7:
  http://www.bltresearch.com/plantab.php
  to the seed companies of the world - a technology which
  replicated the BLT Research Team's early discovery that seeds
  taken from crop circles which had occurred in mature plants grew
  both faster and produced greater yield - was an ongoing effort
  to produce a practical benefit for mankind. Seeds exposed to
  this process did, in fact, grow much more quickly and did
  produce greater yield than un-treated seeds - and they also
  tolerated plant "stressors" (lack of water and/or sunlight) much
  better than normal seed. Unfortunately the "MIR Stressguard"
  technology was not only relatively inexpensive, it caused no
  harmful effects to the environment, thus making it a less
  financially rewarding strategy than the current corporate effort
  which promotes "genetically modified" product.
  John's final efforts were devoted to the development of a
  methodology to accurately calibrate pain levels, particularly
  for use in veterinary practices and for use with people unable
  to communicate.
  His death is a blow to those of us who knew him and to all of us
  struggling to better understand the world around us and the
  strange unknowns that still abound.
  I have written an obituary (below), for those of you who would
  like to include it, and have included his photograph:
  -----
  John A. Burke
  November 13, 1951 - February 26, 2010
  John A. Burke, the "B" in "BLT Research Team Inc." and a native
  of Oyster Bay, Long Island, New York, has died at the age of
  58. An inspired and tireless advocate for the likelihood that
  natural (although currently undiscovered) forces are involved in
  the crop circle phenomenon, John carried out a considerable
  volume of new research during the 1990s, very much "behind the


http://www.ufoupdateslist.com/2010/mar/m05-002.shtml[10/12/2011 14:19:04]
John Burke - The 'B' In 'BLT' Dies

  scenes."
  In 1995 he co-authored the peer-reviewed paper "Semi-Molten
  Meteoric Iron Associated with a Crop Formation" (J. Sci.
  Exploration) which presented evidence that molten iron-rich
  deposits discovered in a 1993 crop circle had been produced
  when meteoric dust, heated through interaction with a
  descending plasma vortex, had impacted the field.
  In conjunction with his crop circle work with Michigan
  biophysicist W.C. Levengood John was instrumental in developing
  the "MIR Stressguard" process, a technique which replicated the
  BLT Team's discovery that seeds taken from crop circles which
  had occurred in mature plants grew both faster than normal and
  also produced increased yield while simultaneously withstanding
  typical plant stressors. Years of attempts by John to bring
  this technology to the world's seed companies failed, because
  the financial profits which could be anticipated were less than
  those garnered by the current, genetic, approach.
  During the last 10 years John had pursued multiple other
  interests and in 2005 he and colleague Kaj Halberg published
  their ground-breaking book "Seed of Knowledge, Stone of Plenty"
  which presented evidence that naturally-occuring earth energies
  had been not only recognized, but utilized, by the ancient
  megalith builders in multiple cultures around the world.
  Reviewed by Boston University geologist Robert M. Schoch, the
  book was praised as "utterly fascinating" and a new "approach
  to understanding the mysteries of ancient megalithic
  structures."
  Most recently John had been working on a method to calibrate
  pain levels so that medical professionals could more accurately
  provide relief of suffering. Animal trials had been found
  effective and testing with humans was being planned.
  John, who had been long divorced, is survived by four sisters
  and one brother, all of whom were with him when he died. John
  was a pioneer=E2=80=A6.and I will miss him very much.
  Nancy Talbott
  BLT Research Team Inc.
  February, 2010
  -----

Sean David Morton Busted By SEC

                                   A mailing list for the study of UFO-related phenomena

                                                Sean David Morton Busted By SEC
  From: UFO UpDates - Toronto <post.nul>
  Date: Fri, 05 Mar 2010 07:56:36 -0500
  Archived: Fri, 05 Mar 2010 07:56:36 -0500
  Subject: Sean David Morton Busted By SEC



   - UFO Watchdog's Royce J Myers III spent years warning of the
  rip-off activities of the self-proclaimed "World's Foremost UFO
  Researcher" Sean David Morton. See:
  http://www.ufowatchdog.com/hall-of-shame/24-sean-david-morton
   - Morton filed a one-million dollar defamation lawsuit against
  Myers and his UFOWatchdog.Com:
  Judge Finds For UFO Watchdog
  http://www.ufowatchdog.com/?view=article&id=28
   - Despite Myers' efforts, conference producers persisted in
  including Morton on their speaker schedules
    - Yesterday the following was published in New York:
  -----
  Source: The New York Post
  http://tinyurl.com/ykspn7l
  March 4, 2010

  SEC Says 'Psychic' Scammed Investors
  By Bruce Golding
  Too bad he couldn't foresee getting caught.
  Federal regulators filed suit today against a self-proclaimed
  psychic who allegedly scammed $6 million by conning suckers into
  believing that his extrasensory abilities would make them "piles
  of money" by trading foreign currencies.
  Sean David Morton - who bills himself as "America's Prophet" -
  "falsely touted his historical success in psychically predicting
  the various rises and falls of the market," according to the
  Securities and Exchange Commission.
  The California-based huckster - whose Web site shows him posing
  with celebs including Sting, Robin Williams and the late Farrah
  Fawcett - solicited investors on late-night radio shows and at
  the 2006 "New Life Expo" in New York City, the Manhattan federal
  court filing says.
  The suit seeks to put an end to Morton's alleged fraud and force
  him to cough up his "ill-gotten gains" along with unspecified
  fines.
  Morton, 51, allegedly lied repeatedly to more than 100 investors
  in his Delphi Investment Group, which the SEC says "was not an
  actual company but was simply a moniker Morton used."



http://www.ufoupdateslist.com/2010/mar/m05-003.shtml[10/12/2011 14:19:04]
Sean David Morton Busted By SEC

  He falsely claimed investor funds would be placed into three
  different accounts called "Vajra Productions," "27 Investments"
  and "Magic Eight Ball Distributing," that were really just shell
  companies, the suit says.
  In reality, the SEC says "he invested only about half of the
  funds with foreign currency trading firms" and diverted the
  rest, including $240,000 that went to a non-profit religious
  organization called the Prophecy Research Institute that he runs
  with his wife, Melissa, who's also named in the suit.
  Morton, who's slated to appear later this month at the New Life
  Expo at the New Yorker Hotel, couldn't immediately be reached
  for comment.

  [Thanks to Greg Boone for the lead]

Charles 'Mogul' Moore - 1920-2010




                                                          UFO Updates
                                    A mailing list for the study of UFO-related phenomena
                                                'Its All Here In Black & White'
    Location: UFOUpDatesList.Com > 2010 > Mar > Mar 6

                                                Charles 'Mogul' Moore - 1920-2010
  From: UFO UpDates - Toronto <post.nul>
  Date: Sat, 06 Mar 2010 08:56:48 -0500
  Archived: Sat, 06 Mar 2010 08:56:48 -0500
  Subject: Charles 'Mogul' Moore - 1920-2010



  Source: New Mexico Tech - Socorro, New Mexico
  http://www.nmt.edu/news/3704-charles-b-moore-1920-2010
  March 4, 2010

  Charles B. Moore, 1920-2010
  New Mexico Tech Notes Passing of Noted Atmospheric Researcher
  [Note: times of services will be posted here when established.
  For an appreciation of Moore's scientific contributions, please
  scroll down.]
  SOCORRO, N.M., March 4, 2010 =96 Charles B. Moore, renowned
  researcher on atmospheric physics, passed away on March 2, 2010,
  in Socorro.
  Moore was professor emeritus of physics at New Mexico Tech and
  former chairman of Tech=92s Langmuir Laboratory for Atmospheric
  Research. Although he retired in 1985, he remained active in his
  research until the last few years, when Alzheimer=92s disease
  affected him. He is survived by his wife, Wilma, and their three
  children, Charles III, Rita, and Malcolm.
  Moore took a long and circuitous route to the heights of his
  profession, managing to bypass a Ph.D. along the way, until
  2003, when he was awarded an honorary Ph.D. by New Mexico Tech.
  He received numerous awards from fellow scientists, including a
  fellowship awarded by the American Geophysical Union, the Otto
  C. Winzen Lifetime Achievement Award of the American Institute
  of Aeronautics and Astronautics, and the Lifetime Achievement
  Award of the Atmospheric Electricity Community.
  Moore was born on October 28, 1920, in Maryville, Tenn. He
  started college at Georgia Institute of Technology in 1940, but
  like many of his generation, his education was interrupted by
  service in World War II. He served as a weather equipment
  officer for the U.S. Army Air Corps in the China-Burma-India
  theater, and later was a weather observer in occupied China.
  Moore returned to Georgia Tech, where he completed his
  bachelor=92s degree in chemical engineering in 1947. He was then
  recruited for Project Mogul by New York University, which
  conducted the project for the U. S. Army Air Corps.
  He later recalled that the extended field trips were what ended
  his graduate schooling.
  Project Mogul involved launching balloons to carry microphones
  up to the base of the stratosphere, where the temperature of the
  atmosphere is highly effective at refracting sound waves. At the
  time, 1947, the United States was concerned with listening for
  nuclear testing by other countries, especially the Soviet Union,
  so the microphone-bearing balloons were launched to listen for
  the sounds.


http://www.ufoupdateslist.com/2010/mar/m06-001.shtml[10/12/2011 14:19:04]
Charles 'Mogul' Moore - 1920-2010


  The experiment succeeded in detecting U.S. nuclear tests in the
  South Pacific, 6,000 miles away, but it also added an important
  footnote to American cultural history. A balloon launched by
  Moore in June of 1947 later proved to be the item that is
  enshrined at Roswell as a =93UFO.=94 Moore didn't realize the part
  he had played in the drama until he happened to see a newspaper
  picture of the pieces of the =93UFO=94 in the 1990s.
  Moore was considered a pioneer in the development and testing of
  modern polyethylene balloons as atmospheric research tools. In
  1947, he made the first flight in a such a balloon, and in a
  later test, he made a 24-hour balloon flight from Minneapolis to
  New Jersey. In 1957, he made a record-breaking flight to the
  altitude of 82,000 feet in a pressurized balloon gondola, with
  Commander Malcolm D. Ross. During this flight, he made the first
  measurements which discovered traces of water vapor in the
  atmosphere of Venus.
  After he carried reconnaissance cameras to high altitude for the
  U.S. Air Force, a program to fly balloons carrying these cameras
  over the Soviet Union was established by General Mills, for whom
  Moore worked until 1953. He was then offered an opportunity to
  work for Arthur D. Little, a research company in Cambridge,
  Mass., again on a project involving research by balloon. While
  there, he designed and built the first alkaline-metal vaporizers
  used in rocket-borne ionospheric probes. Also at Little, Moore
  met and began his long collaboration with Dr. Bernard Vonnegut.
  Vonnegut was well-known as the scientist who had discovered that
  silver iodide could be used for cloud seeding. In 1956, Moore
  and Vonnegut were invited to New Mexico by E. J. Workman, then
  president of New Mexico Tech, to conduct thunderstorm research
  at Mt. Withington, some 70 miles west of Tech. For three
  successive summers, Moore and Vonnegut hauled truckloads of
  equipment to and from Boston each year, until the fateful day in
  1958 when Moore suggested that what they needed was a
  mountaintop lab where the equipment could be kept year round.
  Workman and his colleague Marx Brook liked the idea, but instead
  of Mt. Withington, as Moore proposed, they decided to put their
  lab in the Magdalena Mountains, closer to Socorro and within
  line-of-sight of the campus. By 1964, their lab =96 Langmuir
  Laboratory for Atmospheric Research =96 was built and ready to put
  into operation. In 1965, Dr. Stirling Colgate, the new president
  of New Mexico Tech, offered jobs to both Moore and Vonnegut. The
  latter chose to stay at Arthur D. Little =96 and later moved to
  SUNY Albany =96 but Moore was delighted at the opportunity and
  came to Tech as an associate professor of physics and research
  physicist.
  In 1969, he became the chairman of Langmuir Laboratory. During
  his time at the helm, Moore greatly expanded the lab's
  facilities. He obtained funding for and organized the
  construction of a large addition to the Main Building, a balloon
  hangar, an airplane hangar, and underground shielded rooms
  (Faraday cages) on South Baldy Peak for studying nearby
  lightning. He was also responsible for the construction of a
  vertically-scanning radar and for solving the political problems
  to allow the launching of instrumented rockets into
  thunderstorms over Langmuir Laboratory. In addition, he
  organized the modification and instrumentation of an airplane
  that has flown into thunderstorms for many years.
  Moore also taught techniques of launching balloons in severe
  weather to a number of Tech faculty members and students, many
  of whom continued their work in the field. Thus, he was the
  mentor of many of today's scientists who study electrical
  properties of severe storms.
  Moore nominally retired from New Mexico Tech in 1985, but
  continued to be active in research. He developed the first real
  improvement to the lightning rod since Benjamin Franklin
  invented it in the 18th Century, by proving that blunt-tipped
  rods were more effective than pointed-tipped ones. As a result
  of his work, most of the lightning rods manufactured in the
  United States today are blunt-tipped.
  Moore was a Fellow of the American Geophysical Union and
  received New Mexico Tech's Distinguished Research Award in 1984
  and the Lifetime Achievement Award of the American Institute of


http://www.ufoupdateslist.com/2010/mar/m06-001.shtml[10/12/2011 14:19:04]
Charles 'Mogul' Moore - 1920-2010

  Aeronautics and Astronautics in 1997. He was a fellow in three
  scientific societies: The Royal Meteorological Society, American
  Meteorological Society, and the American Association for the
  Advancement of Science.
  Upon presenting Moore with an honorary doctorate in 2003, Dr.
  Daniel H. Lopez of New Mexico Tech said, =93In a real sense, this
  is not an honorary doctorate at all. Charles B. Moore has been a
  leader in the field of atmospheric physics, and he has done
  research that would have earned him a Ph.D. many times over, had
  he consented to accept one before now.=94
  Dr. Paul Krehbiel, an atmospheric researcher who knew Moore
  well, commented, =93Charlie was a person of many talents. He was a
  pioneer in the study of thunderstorms and lightning and a font
  of scientific knowledge. He was a true student of history and a
  historian in his own right, and possessed an excellent memory.
  He was a mentor and educator of many, myself included. His
  legacy lives on in the ideas, studies and projects that he
  developed and worked on over his long and amazingly varied
  career.=94
  Dr. William Winn, who worked with Charles Moore for many years,
  said, =93Charles inspired me and many other young investigators
  with his enthusiasm, independent thinking, and precise use of
  the English language, and he was a delightful companion because
  of his good humor and storytelling skills."
  -- NMT --
  [Thanks to Joel Carpenter & Current Encounters for the lead]

                                             Re: Charles 'Mogul' Moore - 1920-2010
  From: Bruce Maccabee <brumac.nul>
  Date: Sat, 6 Mar 2010 16:17:00 -0500 (EST)
  Archived: Sat, 06 Mar 2010 19:23:33 -0500
  Subject: Re: Charles 'Mogul' Moore - 1920-2010

  >Source: New Mexico Tech - Socorro, New Mexico
  >http://www.nmt.edu/news/3704-charles-b-moore-1920-2010
  >March 4, 2010

  >Charles B. Moore, 1920-2010
  >New Mexico Tech Notes Passing of Noted Atmospheric Researcher
  The obituary of C.B. Moore brings back memories, of Moore, but
  also of another scientist whose name I have not heard for years:
  E.J. Workman. My memory of him has nothing to do with UFOs
  (which are discussed below).
  During the summer between my sophomore and junior years I got a
  National Science Foundation summer research grant. So for ten
  weeks I did experiments as directed by a professor (Kurt Mayer)
  that concerned the Workman - Reynolds Freezing Potential. This
  was directly connected with research in atmospheric
  electrification.
  W-R, in the late 40's, had investigated the large voltages that
  occur when water freezes. My research involved repeating and
  esxtending some of their research by placing distilled,
  deionized water in a copper half-cylinder - ends sealed - and
  placing one end of the cylinder in a cold liquid.
  I used a bi-filer electrometer to measure the voltage between
  the cylinder and the liquid water contained in it as the water
  froze. I managed to 'set a record' of about 300 volts when I
  used liquid nitrogen as the cooling fluid.
  Of course, the work I did was never published - who cares about
  freezing using liquid nitrogen? But I have never forgotten the W
  - R freezing potential.
  Had I known of Moore's work on electrification I might have
  mentioned it to him when we exchanged letters in the middle
  1980's.
  Regarding Charles B. Moore and his passing, I had some
  correspondence with him in the middle 1980's when I queried him
  about his opinion of his famous, but not mentioned in the
  obituary that listed his achievements... even though his claim
  that the Roswell crash was of a Mogul Balloon was mentioned and
  the April, 1949 sighting of a UFO while he and others were
  tracking a balloon. He was one of several General Mills Balloon
  project personnel who had sightings while tracking balloons in
  the late '40's and early '50s.
  He told me he knew the other witnesses. I asked his opinion of
  his sighting and the sightings of the other GMB project
  witnesses. Although he was not explicit in saying UFOs are
  real, I certainly got the impression that he was not backing
  down on the data generated by his observations and would leave
  the 'Theorizing' to others.



http://www.ufoupdateslist.com/2010/mar/m06-002.shtml[10/12/2011 14:19:05]
Re: Charles 'Mogul' Moore - 1920-2010

  He rejected Donald Menzel's atmospheric refraction theory which
  I have discussed elsewhere. He may have been a 'closet
  believer'!
  For Moore's sighting report and commentary and Menzel's
  'explanation' see the main text and Appendix at:
  http://www.brumac.8k.com/Still In Default/Still in Default.html
  Also in that article are reports from other balloon project
  witnesses.

                                                        Secrecy News -- 03/09/10
  From: Steven Aftergood <saftergood.nul>
  Date: Tue, 09 Mar 2010 12:49:01 -0500
  Archived: Tue, 09 Mar 2010 19:21:06 -0500
  Subject: Secrecy News -- 03/09/10

  SECRECY NEWS
  from the FAS Project on Government Secrecy
  Volume 2010, Issue No. 18
  March 9, 2010
  Secrecy News Blog: http://www.fas.org/blog/secrecy/

  **      PENTAGON STUDY CRITIQUES 2003 TRANSITION IN IRAQ
  **      NAVY INTEL OVERSIGHT, PROTECTING UNCLASSIFIED INFO
  **      BOOK: A GENIUS FOR DECEPTION

  PENTAGON STUDY CRITIQUES 2003 TRANSITION IN IRAQ
  The transition of U.S. military forces in Iraq to post-major combat
  operations in 2003 was marred by failures in leadership and planning,
  according to an internal report prepared for the Pentagon that was
  partially declassified and released this month under the Freedom of
  Information Act.
  "The transition that occurred was not the one that was planned," the 2006
  report delicately stated.
  "Insufficient and untimely availability of resources impeded effectiveness
  of post-combat operations and contributed to a difficult transition."
  Intelligence support, joint command and control, and communications
  infrastructure all "fell short of expectations or needs."
  See "Transitions in Iraq: Changing Environment, Changing Organizations,
  Changing Leadership," Joint Center for Operational Analysis, 21 July
  2006.
  http://www.fas.org/man/eprint/transitions.pdf
  The newly disclosed report was cited in a 2008 book by Lt. Gen.
  Ricardo Sanchez, the former commander of U.S. forces in Iraq.
  According to Gen. Sanchez's account, the report had been
  suppressed at the direction of Defense Secretary Donald
  Rumsfeld, who did not welcome its critical findings.
  http://www.time.com/time/printout/0,8816,1736831,00.html
  In 2008, U.S. Joint Forces Command told TPM Muckraker that the
  report had been completed but was classified and not publicly
  available. ("Pentagon Report on Iraq Debacle 'Remains
  Classified'" by Paul Kiel, May 6, 2008). Now portions of it have
  been released.
  Another newly declassified report found no corroboration of
  allegations that the DoD Joint Forces Intelligence Command
  (JFIC) had withheld information from the 9/11 Commission. The
  DoD Inspector General said there was no basis for such a claim.
  But the 2008 IG report, formerly classified Secret, provides
  some new details on the operation of the JFIC. See "Review of
  Joint Forces Intelligence Command Response to 9/11," September
  23, 2008:
  http://www.fas.org/irp/agency/dod/ig-jfic.pdf


http://www.ufoupdateslist.com/2010/mar/m09-003.shtml[10/12/2011 14:19:07]
Secrecy News -- 03/09/10



  NAVY INTEL OVERSIGHT, PROTECTING UNCLASSIFIED INFO
  The U.S. Navy has released some new guidance pertaining to
  intelligence programs, including the following items.
  "Oversight of the Department of the Navy Military Intelligence
  Program," SECNAV Instruction 5000.38A, February 5, 2010:
  http://www.fas.org/irp/doddir/navy/secnavinst/5000_38a.pdf
  "Required Operational Capabilities and Projected Operational
  Environment for Navy Expeditionary Intelligence Command Forces,"
  OPNAV Instruction 3501.382, March 1, 2010:
  http://www.fas.org/irp/doddir/navy/opnavinst/3501_382.pdf
  The Department of Defense has invited comment on a proposal to
  modify and enhance controls on unclassified DoD information held
  in industry in order to protect such information from
  unauthorized access and disclosure. The proposed changes may be
  altered at a later date, the DoD notice states, in response to
  ongoing development of a government-wide policy on "controlled
  unclassified information." See the March 3 DoD Federal Register
  notice here:
  http://www.fas.org/sgp/news/2010/03/fr030310.html
  The National Aeronautics and Space Administration issued its own
  proposed rule on the handling of "restricted information" in a
  March 4 Federal Register notice:
  http://www.fas.org/sgp/news/2010/03/fr030410.html

  BOOK: A GENIUS FOR DECEPTION
  One of the few unclassified discussions of official U.S. policy
  on the use of "cover stories" to conceal classified activities
  and operations advised that "Cover stories must be believable"
  (1992 draft SAP Supplement, at p. 3-1-5):
  http://www.fas.org/sgp/library/nispom/sapsup-draft92.pdf
  But such pedestrian guidance would not have been needed by
  British military and intelligence officials during the past
  century because they had an almost instinctive gift for
  concealment and misdirection, writes Nicholas Rankin in "A
  Genius for Deception: How Cunning Helped the British Win Two
  World Wars" (Oxford University Press, 2009).
  http://amazon.com/Genius-Deception-Cunning-Helped-British/dp/019538704X
  From the emergence of camouflage (a word that entered the
  English language in 1917) to the development of modern
  propaganda to the strategic deceptions of World War II, the
  author treats familiar figures such as T.E. Lawrence and John
  Buchan (author of The 39 Steps) and many unfamiliar ones.
  "A Genius for Deception" is surprisingly colorful, with an
  endless stream of strange, offbeat and sometimes appalling
  anecdotes that the author has culled from his extensive reading
  and research.
  He quotes an enterprising British intelligence officer in World
  War I who discovered that the German officers' latrines in an
  East Africa camp "were a good source of soiled documents and
  letters, yielding 'filthy, though accurate information'."
  In a personal epilogue, Rankin observes that the calculated
  deception of an enemy is ethically distinct from and not to be
  confused with propaganda directed at one's own people.

Glyph-Like Radar Patterns Over Oz




                                                          UFO Updates
                                    A mailing list for the study of UFO-related phenomena
                                                'Its All Here In Black & White'
    Location: UFOUpDatesList.Com > 2010 > Mar > Mar 11

                                               Glyph-Like Radar Patterns Over Oz
  From: Colin Andrews <cprandrews.nul>
  Date: Wed, 10 Mar 2010 22:17:57 +0000 (UTC)
  Archived: Thu, 11 Mar 2010 07:37:23 -0500
  Subject: Glyph-Like Radar Patterns Over Oz

  What Turned A 1,000 Year Super Drought Into A 100 Year
  Super Storm Over Melbourne, Australia?
  By Colin Andrews
  C.P.R. International
  Melbourne has never seen anything like it, thats official.
  Visitors to this website know that for nearly two months I have
  been reporting on strange and inexplicable radar patterns that
  have appeared on the Australian government Bureau of Meteorology
  (BOM) website. An extensive range of designs has been observed
  since January: radial spokes, spiral arms and doughnut patterns
  have appeared like glyphs from some amazing aboriginal art work.
  I inquired directly with the BOM agency asking for an
  explanation. The agency pokesperson responded by providing
  suggested causes for some of the interference patterns that had
  appeared in the radar transmissions, while admitting that the
  agency was attempting to resolve what they called the other
  "unknown interferences".
  Amongst the most bizarre of these patterns consistently showed
  up over Melbourne. The patterns have been appearing on a regular
  basis across the entire continent and seem to impact weather
  conditions when they arrive. The entire ensemble looks like a
  chess game being played by someone manipulating weather
  conditions over Australia.
  Full article:
  http://www.colinandrews.net/Melbourne-DroughtToSuperstorm-HAARP.html

Re: The Biggest Crop Circle Ever: The Reality Show

                                    A mailing list for the study of UFO-related phenomena

                                   Re: The Biggest Crop Circle Ever: The Reality Show
  From: William Treurniet <wtreurniet.nul>
  Date: Thu, 11 Mar 2010 11:08:32 -0500
  Archived: Thu, 11 Mar 2010 11:13:43 -0500
  Subject: Re: The Biggest Crop Circle Ever: The Reality Show

  >News Links - 07-03-10
  >The Biggest Crop Circle Ever: The Reality Show
  >http://tinyurl.com/yfgfu42

  Here we go again - some people made a crop formation with
  stomping boards, hence all formations are made with stomping
  boards.
  The author, who seems to want to believe this, says that 60
  people were involved, and that everything was recorded on video.
  The video merely shows one person stomping a few meters of crop
  in the dark. The implication is that the entire formation was
  created in the dark. If so, it's unlikely that the whole
  operation was recorded on video as claimed. We are asked to
  believe that the formation seen in daylight was the result.
  It's possible that 60 people in the field could have made the
  pattern in a relatively short time using some technique to lay
  out the pattern. However, how this was done was never
  demonstrated. Maybe one person positioned on the chest of the
  figure aimed a laser pointer to mark the lines which appear to
  radiate from that point. In that case, the darkness might
  actually have been an asset.
  Perhaps they could have gone that extra mile and irradiated each
  circle with a microwave point source so that the plant nodes
  would stretch in proportion to the distance from the source. Or
  simulated some of the other effects measured in crop formations
  that go beyond mere flattening of the crops such as changes in
  soil properties. How about twisting and interleaving undamaged
  crop swatches in artistic patterns throughout the formation, or
  flattening crops too brittle to step on without breaking?
  It is unclear from the video why the formation was made. If it
  was just to produce a work of art, then why do it in the dark?
  If it was to suggest that all formations are made with stomping
  boards, then why stop at mere flattening of the crop? I guess
  the answer would be that most people are uninformed enough to be
  easily fooled.
  Perhaps someone who has a better understanding of the Dutch
  language than I do can extract more information from the video.

  William Treurniet

                                                        Secrecy News -- 03/11/10
  From: Steven Aftergood <saftergood.nul>
  Date: Thu, 11 Mar 2010 11:57:57 -0500
  Archived: Thu, 11 Mar 2010 12:11:14 -0500
  Subject: Secrecy News -- 03/11/10

  SECRECY NEWS
  from the FAS Project on Government Secrecy
  Volume 2010, Issue No. 19
  March 11, 2010
  Secrecy News Blog: http://www.fas.org/blog/secrecy/

  ** WHITE HOUSE PROMOTES PRIZES FOR OPEN GOVERNMENT
  ** ARMY: FOIA REQUESTERS ARE "NOT AN ADVERSARY"
  ** CONGRESSIONAL OVERSIGHT, AND MORE FROM CRS

  WHITE HOUSE PROMOTES PRIZES FOR OPEN GOVERNMENT
  Executive branch agencies should "increase the use of prizes and
  challenges as tools for promoting open government," the White
  House Office of Management and Budget said in a memorandum to
  agency heads this week.
  "It is Administration policy to strongly encourage agencies to
  utilize prizes and challenges as tools for advancing open
  government, innovation, and the agency's mission," OMB said.
  http://www.fas.org/sgp/obama/wh030810.pdf
  The memorandum, as promised in the December 2009 White House
  Open Government Directive, is intended to provide "a framework
  for how agencies can use challenges, prizes, and other
  incentive-backed strategies to find innovative or cost-effective
  solutions to improving open government."
  http://www.fas.org/sgp/obama/opengov.pdf
  The substance of the desired improvements was not spelled out in
  the latest memo, but the earlier Directive said that "The three
  principles of transparency, participation, and collaboration
  form the cornerstone of an open government." None of these
  principles is instinctive or can be taken for granted, and the
  prize program is an evidently sincere effort to help overcome
  bureaucratic resistance to greater openness.
  "A prize should not be an end in itself, but one means within a
  broader strategy for spurring private innovation and change,"
  the new OMB memo said.

  ARMY: FOIA REQUESTERS ARE "NOT AN ADVERSARY"
  Slowly and unevenly, the Obama Administration's open government
  message is filtering down to the agency level.
  We have entered "a new era of open government," Army officials
  informed a government audience recently. There will be
  "increased emphasis on the Freedom of Information Act... Agency
  FOIA programs must be improved... Commanders need to direct all
  agency personnel to place a higher priority on timely assistance
  to FOIA personnel."



http://www.ufoupdateslist.com/2010/mar/m11-007.shtml[10/12/2011 14:19:11]
Secrecy News -- 03/11/10

  The FOIA requester "is not an adversary," the Army FOIA
  Management Conference was told, according to November 2009
  briefing slides that were released last month.
  http://www.fas.org/sgp/othergov/dod/army-foia.pdf
  In reality, many FOIA proceedings are quite adversarial. But
  perhaps the Army meant that both FOIA requesters and FOIA
  responders are part of the same process, and therefore ought to
  cooperate as far as possible. It's a wholesome message to send.

  CONGRESSIONAL OVERSIGHT, AND MORE FROM CRS
  New Congressional Research Service reports obtained by Secrecy
  News that have not been made readily available to the public
  include the following.
  "Visa Security Policy: Roles of the Departments of State and
  Homeland Security," March 8, 2010:
  http://www.fas.org/sgp/crs/homesec/R41093.pdf
  "Legislative Options After Citizens United v. FEC: Constitutional and
  Legal Issues," March 8, 2010:
  http://www.fas.org/sgp/crs/misc/R41096.pdf
  "FY2011 Budget Documents: Internet and GPO Availability," March 8, 2010:
  http://www.fas.org/sgp/crs/misc/R41095.pdf
  "House Committee Markups: Manual of Procedures and Procedural Strategies,"
  February 25, 2010:
  http://www.fas.org/sgp/crs/misc/R41083.pdf
  "Congressional Oversight: An Overview," February 22, 2010:
  http://www.fas.org/sgp/crs/misc/R41079.pdf

  Yes, that would be impressive if he was in an Air Force uniform,
  but that is actually a CAP uniform. For those who don't know,
  the CAP is a civilian auxiliary of the Air Force, but they are
  volunteers who serve without pay and are not in the Air Force.
  Yes, there are additional pictures of him in uniform, but he has
  removed the distinctive CAP insignia from the lapels. He is
  wearing a combination of CAP ribbons and military medals, which
  is legal on a CAP uniform but not on an Air Force uniform.


http://www.ufoupdateslist.com/2010/mar/m11-008.shtml[10/12/2011 14:19:12]
Re: Pentagon UFO Crash Retrieval Officer Speaks Out


  So, the pictures prove nothing, other than he was in the CAP.
  They do not prove that he was in the Air Force.
  >Isn't it a felony to claim to have been an officer in the U.S.
  >military and to get VA and military benefits? I can ask family
  >and friends at the Pentagon and VA. They should have clear cut
  >records.
  It is a crime under the Stolen Valor Act to wear awards to which
  you are not entitled and to claim to have been an officer in the
  Armed Forces of the United States... which we now refer to in a
  politically correct climate as the Uniformed Services.
  I have attempted to get records from a variety of sources and
  have found that Willingham was an enlisted soldier in the Army,
  rose to the rank of E4, so he wasn't even an NCO, and his
  service was just about fourteen months. There is nothing for his
  claimed Air Force service, no record of flight training at any
  of the bases, no record of his claimed Korean service, no
  records of his claimed injuries in Korea, no record of him in
  the Armed Services Registry, no DD 214s, no suggestion of any
  military service beyond his short tour in the Army.
  >In case there are missing or questionable records take into
  >account another witness I've dug up who was part of an elite
  >interceptor group during the late 40's into the early 60's.
  >Looking into his and his colleague's military service was tough
  >as the records were hidden. The problem with hiding his
  >particular records is that he later went on to become a

  >they were newsworthy and part of many public records. He was in

  many >social events and hundreds if not thousands of family and
  civic >photos and diaries etc. So when told he didn't do this
  and >wasn't at that, thanks to folks keeping photos and films it
  >totally invalidated what the government said in abundance.
  Yes, but you found records... there are none to support the
  claims made by Willingham. The few documents he has supplied are
  apparent forgeries. The entries on them are improper, just the
  sort of thing you would expect if someone was attempting to
  prove long military service.
  Now, I did attempt to get my own Air Force records but the
  document they returned was so full of errors that they were
  laughable. However, I have loads of documents from my military
  service so that I can prove all my claims. This guy has
  presented nothing and he claims that he was denied a pension
  because he talked about UFOs.
  >With your officer's status getting data on Willingham should be
  >easy from simple present and past administrative records. If
  >Willingham wasn't military then someone should be looking into
  >why he's receiving, if any, benefits. Is he getting officer's
  >benefits or non comm?
  Ahhh, here's the rub. If I press forward on this, suggesting
  that he was illegally denied a pension, and there are no
  supporting documents, then this guy is in legal trouble. I have
  seen enough, from the faked records to the pictures in which he
  is clearly attempting to deceive, that I can say he was never an
  Air Force officer, and if that is true, then his story is bogus.
  But I just don't want to get him into real trouble because
  he's in his mid-80s, in somewhat poor health, and is harming
  no one, other than those who believe his tale. After Kaufmann
  and Anderson, I have become very careful with this.
  And yes, I'm passionate about this because I am tired of people
  claiming military service that they don't have as a way of
  gaining credibility. Who is going to listen to a guy talk about
  a UFO crash that he alone has talked about... unless he was an
  Air Force colonel on some mission. Not the case here.

P/2010 A2




                                                          UFO Updates
                                   A mailing list for the study of UFO-related phenomena
                                               'Its All Here In Black & White'
    Location: UFOUpDatesList.Com > 2010 > Mar > Mar 11

                                                                    P/2010 A2
  From: William Treurniet <wtreurniet.nul>
  Date: Thu, 11 Mar 2010 16:07:58 -0500
  Archived: Thu, 11 Mar 2010 17:03:05 -0500
  Subject: P/2010 A2

  Back in January, 2010, the Hubble space telescope took a picture
  of a comet-like object called P/2010 A2. The object is unusual
  and scientists speculated it is the result of the collision
  between two asteroids. There is a main nucleus lying outside a
  halo of dust. The nucleus is estimated to be 460 feet in
  diameter.
  http://www.nasa.gov/mission_pages/hubble/science/asteroid-20100202.html
  I would like to offer an alternative speculation. There is a
  partial halo pattern overlaying the main nucleus, and this same
  toroidal pattern I have seen many times before in earth-bound
  photos of UFOs. I would suggest that the main nucleus is, in
  fact, a spaceship of sorts. Please go to
  http://www.treurniet.ca/tori/ufoindex.htm
  and select P2010_A2 from the end of the list on that page.
  Notice that the main object of P/2010 A2 is overlaid by a
  partial toroidal shape made barely visible by the debris in the
  vicinity. The enhanced image shows this shape more clearly. I
  suggest that this debris was organized by some kind of force
  field originating at the main nucleus. Something similar is seen
  in Figure 1 of
  http://www.treurniet.ca/tori/toriphys/toriphys.htm
  where smoke and ashes from a forest fire is organized into a
  toroidal shape.
  The other images indexed on the same page as P/2010 A2 all show
  UFOs in proximity to the same kind of toroidal shape. The UFOs
  are consistently located on or near the ring, never in the
  center of the torus. Such consistency suggests that the tori are
  visible properties of a propulsion system used by these
  vehicles. Perhaps it works something like the Alcubierre warp
  drive. Notice also that the ring in P/2010 A2 appears to be
  aligned with the velocity vector.
  Although NASA would not dare to admit the possibility, I would
  say this evidence suggests that P/2010 A2 is a powered spaceship
  surrounded by debris rather than dead asteroid fragments.

  William Treurniet

                                           Re: Pentagon UFO Crash Retrieval Officer
  From: Steve Kaeser <steve.nul>
  Date: Fri, 12 Mar 2010 12:45:21 -0500
  Archived: Fri, 12 Mar 2010 12:56:03 -0500
  Subject: Re: Pentagon UFO Crash Retrieval Officer

  >From: Greg Boone <Evolbaby.nul>
  >To: post.nul
  >Date: Fri, 12 Mar 2010 11:23:18 EST
  >Subject: Re: Pentagon UFO Crash Retrieval Officer Speaks Out
  >[snip]
  >Yes you say they're clearly forgeries and I'm betting you should
  >know authentic documents in this case, however, getting an
  >independent examination best serves the public and researchers
  >who aren't experts. One explanation you've covered already
  >regarding your own records containing errors. One researcher
  >reminded me of the big fire in Missouri years ago that destroyed
  >many records from that era. Knowing our government, trying to
  >restore those documents would lead to tons of errors but that's
  >going way outside the ballpark here but is something to take
  >into account. Bottom line is to take his documentation, have
  >them examined and have him take his best shot at an explanation.
  Greg,
  The National Records Center fire in St. Louis is often cited by
  the military as a reason that records cannot be found, but often
  those records are duplicated elsewhere. I worked with Veterans
  when I was employment as a Congressional Aide and had to look
  for secondary sources for Veterans Benefits, and they later
  acknowledged that Jesse Marcel Sr.'s military records were NOT
  destroyed in the fire as they'd originally said in a written
  response. The "Fire" has become an easy answer for them to use
  when they can't locate a record or want to get someone off their
  back. The records are primarily used to verify claims by
  veterans for benefits, so I doubt a lot of errors have been
  introduced, and those records that were destroyed are very
  limited in detals at this point. On the other hand, the identity
  of the records burned is fairly well documented.
  But we appear to have someone making extraordinary claims, and
  that should require extraordinary verification. Let's not get to
  the point of having to prove that he's a fraud, if he fails to
  prove his own legitimacy.

  Steve

  in society.

  justify such melodrama. Chances are, folks, it's just harmless
  fibbing.

  this kind of psychological excess serves to reinforce the
  stereotype of all ufologists as paranoid crazies.

  >when I was employment as a Congressional Aide and had to look
  >for secondary sources for Veterans Benefits, and they later
  >acknowledged that Jesse Marcel Sr.'s military records were NOT
  >destroyed in the fire as they'd originally said in a written
  >response. The "Fire" has become an easy answer for them to use
  >when they can't locate a record or want to get someone off their

  Now you're talkin'! I agree about the Missouri records. I also
  have found that where one set of records or documents are
  missing there are duplicates or corrobrating documents from
  another source such as school enrollment records as folks do
  enroll their kids in such activities as schools, social groups.
  Also municipal documents such as real estate purchases, bank
  verification, even parking and traffic tickets.
  Everyone leaves a trail of bread crumbs. Some to the point they
  can't be all missing or covered up as was seen in a recent
  eyewitness I've found. There was a sorry attempt to hide his
  records but whomever was behind it totally, totally missed a
  tsunami of public and private sources of verification of
  location and activities.

  away to avoid confusion and folks running off on wild goose
  chases.

  foremost experts in these areas. I can take his data, forward it
  to them and they'll validate, invalidate, or find inconclusive.
  That's all we're doing here but I do appreciate that you can see
  the Missouri fire records and how that situation has been
  handled as I can agree having run into that situation on other
  subject matters regarding veteran's rights and benefits. Without
  being in contact we both have found the same things thus
  corroboration to a degree.

  I really don't question that XL D-Sign did it, but I do wonder
  about the motivation. Were they contracted by the TV company to
  do it? Why was it done in the dark? Wouldn't this have increased
  the degree of difficulty?
  Doing it in the dark suggests an agenda of "proving" that all
  crop formations (most apparently made in the dark) were created
  using a similar technique. If that was the real purpose, then
  why was only board stomping discussed and not how the layout and
  construction was done in the dark?
  In your discussion, you seem to fall into line with such an
  agenda by saying that other complex formations like the Julia
  set were probably stomped "free-hand". You close your article by
  saying
  "The biggest crop circle ever created was recorded by a reality
  TV show, and will soon be featured by a documentary produced by
  its creators. Most people will not believe that that has
  anything to do with the “mystery” of crop circles."
  Could it be that "most people" would be right? So far, I have
  seen nothing in the XL Design project that addresses the
  experiential and scientific anomalies that have been widely
  reported.

  reported to the local FBI office. Stolen Valor, whether service
  or awards, impacts on those who earned such recognition.
  The records destroyed in the National Records Center fire in St.
  Louis are fairly well documented. See:
  http://www.archives.gov/st-louis/military-personnel/fire-1973.html
  My grandfather's Army records for his service in WW I (1917-
  1918) are missing. George Matthews was born the year after the
  Battle of the Little Big Horn (1876) and was somewhat an old
  fart to be enlisting in the Army. He died in 1958, when I was


http://www.ufoupdateslist.com/2010/mar/m13-006.shtml[10/12/2011 14:19:22]
Re: Pentagon UFO Crash Retrieval Officer

  nine years old. See:
  http://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Battle_of_the_Little_Bighorn . I
  did find a service card on file with the State of Indiana. Also,
  proof of his service with the an association of the 42nd
  Infantry (Rainbow) Division. See:
  http://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/42nd_Infantry_Division_(United_States)
  My father's WW II Navy records provided by the NRC are somewhat
  incomplete. When he died in 1966, the DVA (Department of
  Veterans Affairs) stated his medical file was received; however,
  in snail mail and e-mail correspondence I have yet to locate
  these.
  My Army records (1970-90) are fairly complete. I learned
  recently my unit received two medals in Vietnam I didn't know
  about. So it is a mixed bag and varies on a case-by-case basis.
  Again, Stolen Valor, whether in the context of UFOs or not, is
  serious business. Chuck W., one of members on the Thailand-Laos-
  Cambodia Brotherhood forum is a retired FBI agent. He has
  suggested all cases of Stolen Valor be reported.

  Terry

  >dark. (Although this was know twenty or more years ago, but
  >people still seem unable to accept the fact.)
  Perhaps people don't accept that fact because they are not
  simple-minded enough. It's not hard to accept that the stomping
  technique can flatten crops. Most people could imagine doing
  such a thing themselves. However, the technique could not
  produce some of the artistic lays often seen in crop formations
  as well as many other observed anomalies. So this particular
  demonstration clears up nothing at all. Much more than the
  flattening technique is a mystery.
  >>If that was the real purpose, then
  >>why was only board stomping discussed and not how the layout and
  >>construction was done in the dark?
  >Commercial confidentiality. Why tell any competitors about your
  >techniques? Try asking Coca Cola for their 'secret formula'!
  So the reason for doing it in the dark was to prevent outsiders
  from seeing the techniques employed? But if it were done in
  daylight, such special proprietary techniques would not be
  necessary! Even I could come up with a scheme in daylight to
  manage the activities of the 60 people involved. There's got to
  be another reason why darkness was a requirement. The most
  obvious is the one I suggested above - a misguided belief that
  that would prove something about how crop formations are made.

  William
