  I must be missing something - where is the evidence that the
  video in January showing some kids having some fun in the dark,
  was comprised of footage taken in a certain field on or shortly
  before 7 Aug. 2009? The video:
  http://tinyurl.com/yfgfu42
  showed the excellent aerial shot of the formation, but as far as
  I can determine, showed nothing to connect it with a group of
  hoaxers at work in that field on or before 7 August. They failed
  to show the field at the start of their work, and during various
  stages of its construction in that same field as it supposedly
  was being hoaxed. This could easily have been done by the
  standard means of the use of a camera atop a long pole, plus
  spotlight illumination. Surely the burden of proof in a claimed
  hoax lies with the hoaxers.
  Perhaps by claiming they had hoaxed it at night they felt they
  could get by without showing any of this.
  One wonders, too, how such a large construction in the dark
  could have been accomplished by hoaxers with zero obvious
  mistakes, and without any prior practice sessions having been
  undertaken in nearby fields. The same question of course holds
  for all the large, elaborate crop-circle formations in England
  over the past 10 or 15 years.
  The shots of a few would-be hoaxers looking at large tracings of
  the pattern, and sections thereof, on engineering paper in that
  video reminds me of similar shots back in the summer of 1990
  from a video of the giant, dry-lake-bed sri-yantra pictograph in
  the rather remote area east of Steens Mountain in eastern Oregon
  (UFO Magazine 6, No. 3, 1991). At least their video showed the
  after-the-fact claimed hoaxers in the proper area, studying an
  engineering drawing of the sri yantra supposedly just before
  getting to work hand-pulling a plow. After the smoking guns in
  the video were pointed out to their chief claimant, that video
  was phased out and replaced by another in which the fatal flaws
  were omitted. Only later did a notable UFO report in the same
  region come to light that could explain the source of the
  pictograph.

  Hey Cathy & Listers,
  I'll take a shot at answering that. It's Fraud.
  Military service is not equal to going a couple of weeks without
  ice cream. It's incredibly difficult, both mentally and
  physically, and extremely dangerous - and that's just during
  training, ignoring the fact that your job is to be shot at by
  hostile strangers.
  When you've actually climbed Mount Everest (metaphorically
  speaking) you resent when some 300lb slob whose ass has never
  risen further than a bar stool claims he's done the same.
  Falsely claiming military service, or decoration, (lovely term,
  stealing valour) is no different than claiming you are a doctor
  or lawyer without going through the necessary schooling and
  accreditation.
  Further, here in Canada, it is illegal to wear any military
  decoration unless it was awarded to you. Which means, no you
  can't wear your father's decorations, even if it's to honor him.
  It's fraud. You're pretending to be something you're not.
  And in the states, if Congress gets past the healthcare
  schmoozle, there is a bill pending that would make it illegal to
  verbally claim military service, let alone wear medals you
  haven't earned.
  I served in the Canadian military and I agree with this whole-
  heartedly.
  I hate phonies, but those who steal valour are cowardly scum who
  should be outted as the liars they are. I consider it the
  smallest amount of respect we can pay to those men and women who
  have or are putting their lives on the line.
  The right to call your self a veteran comes at a very high
  price, too high for phonies to be allowed to degrade or besmirch
  it.
  Sorry about the rant,

  >Subject: Re: The Biggest Crop Circle Ever: The Reality Show
  >>From: William Treurniet <wtreurniet.nul>
  >>To: post.nul
  >>Date: Sat, 13 Mar 2010 11:36:50 -0500
  >>Subject: Re: The Biggest Crop Circle Ever: The Reality Show
  >>So the reason for doing it in the dark was to prevent outsiders
  >>from seeing the techniques employed? But if it were done in
  >>daylight, such special proprietary techniques would not be
  >>necessary!
  >Maybe they want to hide their techniques for making the so-
  >called 'layering' effects?
  >>Even I could come up with a scheme in daylight to
  >>manage the activities of the 60 people involved. There's got to
  >>be another reason why darkness was a requirement. The most
  >>obvious is the one I suggested above - a misguided belief that
  >>that would prove something about how crop formations are made.
  >So by making complex crop designs in the dark, you're not
  >actually proving that people can make complex crop designs in
  >the dark?
  Sure you are. But what would be the reason for making the job
  harder than it needs to be? Proving that a circle can be made by
  stomping could just as well have been done in daylight. The
  created circles did not appear to have the amazing properties
  that have been reported, so in that sense, it was not comparable
  to the others anyway.
  >The desperation of some people in clinging on to belief in
  >magical crop circles is amusing if not particularly edifying. I
  >think it's all part of an anti-human mind set which believes that
  >as a species we are a pretty miserable lot, and that almost
  >anything, including fields of cereal crops, is smarter than we
  >are.
  >Personally I think humanity is under-rated.
  I've said it several times already but I guess I need to say it
  again. I can accept that these people did make the crop
  formation as advertised. I just think this has no bearing on how
  many of the crop formations are created.
  Why can't some people understand that this accomplishment does
  not illuminate why various physical anomalies in formations have
  appeared over the years?
  The most compelling for me is Haselhoff's report that plant node
  lengthening was proportional to the distance of the plant from
  the center of the circle (correlation= 0.988). Levengood and
  Talbott found the same thing in three different formations.
  Certainly, this can't be a result of the stomping technique.


http://www.ufoupdateslist.com/2010/mar/m14-008.shtml[10/12/2011 14:19:31]

  Why would you persist in ignoring such effects? Who is the
  desperate believer here?

  William

  choose.

  celebrities do, you can. If you say you've won a Battle Star,
  you can. We can walk down the street wearing U.S. Navy on our t-

  durable and we_the_people are the owners.

  relatives got medals and some didn't but sure deserved it. One
  thing I've learned is be respectful of folks in general because
  you just don't know who has served and done what. It's common
  for us to find out that folks we know did deeds of daring do and
  were military and we had no clue they had earned medals and
  rank. Some can't mention it because of the secrecy of their
  missions.

  provide those who truly did earn their medals and rank. It
  impacts the resources of their families and caregivers who need
  a hand too.

  who has come forth and stated amazing stories regarding UFOs and
  has offered up documentation that some have stated are forgeries
  and fake. It's our job to do the simple task of gathering these
  documents and having them cross checked by professionals in that
  field and that's that. Should the man's claims prove fraudulent
  then it's up to the authorities to handle them as they see fit.
  Right now, like all U.S. citizens he's innocent until proven
  guilty.

  >spotlight illumination. Surely the burden of proof in a claimed
  >hoax lies with the hoaxers.
  My take on UFOs in general is that some very powerful entity, I
  won't speculate who, doesn't want the full truth about UFOs
  known, and apparently doesn't want archaeological finds which
  contradict as-taught-in-school history known about either.
  That is my theory, based on many years of news stories in
  various arenas.
  If that is so, then very likely, the true origin of those crop
  glyphs which contain elements which can't be duplicated by human
  hoaxers is also wanted to _not_ be made known.
  Is it possible, the "reality show" which "created" this huge
  glyph had a crew at the ready, and when a huge glyph was
  discovered, actually videotaped a hoaxed "creation," on top of a
  genuine non- human-hoaxed glyph?
  Of course with video, it would be "duck soup" to videotape 60
  people bustling around in a farm field at night and then cut to
  an actual non-hoaxed glyph.
  I'm in the camp which is asking to see the layout details.
  Having experience in both drafting and surveying, I don't
  believe that 60 people could create a glyph of that size in the
  dark. I believe that there would be so much unintended stomping
  of the non- glyph crop that it would be a mess when daylight
  arrived.
  Let's see them demonstrate, in daylight, their claimed layout
  procedures.

  Eleanor White

  What you as a believer should support:
  Let some scientists collect samples from various crop circles
  around the world.
  Let some scientific laboratories analyse the samples.
  Have their reports and results printed in scientific magazines
  for discussion among other scientists.
  And do it all over again, and again, and again........
  Then we might have something to discuss.
  Our discussions here of various aspects of the stories about
  crop circles is worth nothing.
  And again: Go out and try for yourself, before you argue about
  what can be done in the middle of the night with a stomping
  technique...

  Best regards from Denmark
  Ole

New 3D Software & UFO Imagery




                                                          UFO Updates
                                   A mailing list for the study of UFO-related phenomena
                                               'Its All Here In Black & White'
    Location: UFOUpDatesList.Com > 2010 > Mar > Mar 16

                                                 New 3D Software & UFO Imagery
  From: Greg Boone <Evolbaby.nul>
  Date: Tue, 16 Mar 2010 00:05:33 EDT
  Archived: Tue, 16 Mar 2010 10:11:06 -0400
  Subject: New 3D Software & UFO Imagery

  With 3D movies all the rage today I figured I would pass some
  old UFO images including video and pics through the new software
  processors to see what they would glean.
  If no one futzes around with the basic image then one gets a
  good look at size and distance and relationships to other
  objects in these images. Since we have numerous reproductions of
  such classics as Billy Meier's images and more, we should be
  able to get a real good idea of how large and how far objects in
  these videos and pics are.
  Models and forced perspective appear just as they do so it's
  easy to see if they're fake or not. Even the new 3D special
  effects movies are easy to detect.
  So if no one has done so before, you might take some time and
  see if this works. I've got my own crew working on some images
  and will see what comes from them.

  Best,
  Greg
  ufomafia.com

Col. Willingham's Testimony - Pt. II




                                                          UFO Updates
                                       A mailing list for the study of UFO-related phenomena
                                                   'Its All Here In Black & White'
    Location: UFOUpDatesList.Com > 2010 > Mar > Mar 16

                                                Col. Willingham's Testimony - Pt. II
   From: Greg Boone <Evolbaby.nul>
   Date: Tue, 16 Mar 2010 02:36:45 EDT
   Archived: Tue, 16 Mar 2010 10:15:22 -0400
   Subject: Col. Willingham's Testimony - Pt. II

   Well, Robert Willingham got on Rense's program tonight and
   elaborated on his exploits.
   The big issue of his whereabouts and associations during the
   1950's regarding the Air Force and Air Force Reserves got
   touched on lightly and not enough for my satisfaction and I've
   asked Rense to ask his authors to expound on that data.
   However, a few surprises did arise that adds fuel to the fire.
   Willingham mentioned that he was very close friends with late
   President Lyndon Johnson and Willingham's authors stated they've
   seen the awards and so-forth from Johnson and Congress bestowed
   upon Willingham.
   Also, Willingham mentioned W. Todd Zechel whose data some of you
   already know as one of his main contacts from the Pentagon and
   who had been working on a book containing Willingham's exploits.
   Zechel is important as he'd made history several times outside
   of the UFO field especially in groundbreaking legal successes
   against the CIA with FOIA actions that clearly showed how much
   they lie and stonewall in direct disregard to the courts. Zechel
   was a one man gang in regard to changing the face of how FOIA
   should be done.
   Added to that, Willingham again stated he'd give up some film
   and pics to his authors and Rense regarding UFOs.
   Stories like these call for so much extra footwork and one to
   sharpen one's skeptical eye and gauge whether to pursue. Lots of
   claims, some documentation, and logic and integrity calls for
   further validation before an extra ear gets bent in his
   direction.
   So the original issue of his documentation was addressed
   somewhat but not fully. However, new information has surfaced
   from him that adds more documentation from sources easy to
   check. More legwork is needed to establish who, what, and where.
   Then we should listen to more of his testimony and not before
   says I.

   Best,
   Greg
   ufomafia.com
   sightings.com

Tributes For NZ Space Scientist




                                                          UFO Updates
                                   A mailing list for the study of UFO-related phenomena
                                               'Its All Here In Black & White'
    Location: UFOUpDatesList.Com > 2010 > Mar > Mar 17

                                                   Tributes For NZ Space Scientist
  From: Geoff Blackmore <geoff_184.nul>
  Date: Thu, 18 Mar 2010 03:07:53 +1300
  Archived: Wed, 17 Mar 2010 10:34:48 -0400
  Subject: Tributes For NZ Space Scientist

  Source: Stuff
  http://tinyurl.com/ykcmm9l
  17/03/2010
  Tributes For NZ Space Scientist - Sir William Ian Axford
  NZPA
  Tributes flowed today for renowned New Zealand space scientist
  Sir William Ian Axford, who died at his Napier home at the
  weekend after a long illness. He was 77.
  Sir Ian, who is survived by his wife Joy and four adult
  children, worked on many American and European space probes,
  such as Voyager and Giotto designing robot craft and calculating
  orbits.
  "His achievements were not only as a researcher, but also as a
  leader of science organisations," said Garth Carnaby, president
  of New Zealand's science academy, the Royal Society.
  "Sir Ian was one of New Zealand's most remarkable scientists and
  had a distinguished international career".
  Sir Ian was widely regarded as a "rock star" of New Zealand
  science, and the society awarded him its top science honour, the
  Rutherford Medal, in 1995, "for his excellent contribution to
  fundamental research which has led to a deeper understanding of
  the nature of planetary magnetospheres, comets, interplanetary
  space, the behaviour of interstellar gas and the origin of
  cosmic rays."
  At the end of 1995, he was made a Knight Bachelor - one of the
  last under the imperial system before the introduction of New
  Zealand honours - one year on from having been named New
  Zealander of the Year.
  Born in Dannevirke, January 2, 1933, Sir Ian was educated at
  Napier Boys' High, and attended university at Canterbury,
  Manchester and Cambridge, where he took his PhD in 1960.
  He worked in defence research in Britain, Canada and New Zealand
  before spending 11 years in the United States, first at Cornell
  University as professor of astronomy and then at the University
  of California at San Diego where he was professor of physics and
  applied physics.
  In 1974, he worked for eight years for the Max Planck Institute
  for Aeronomy in Germany, which later became the Max Planck
  Institute for Solar System Research.
  He returned in 1985 as a director overseeing the successful
  Giotto space mission to Halley's Comet in 1986.
  Sir Ian was president of Cospar, the Space Research Committee of
  the International Council of Scientific Unions, and of the
  European Geophysical Society, and vice-president of the Asia-


http://www.ufoupdateslist.com/2010/mar/m17-005.shtml[10/12/2011 14:19:46]
Tributes For NZ Space Scientist

  Oceania Geophysical Society.
  He received numerous honours including the Space Science Award
  by the American Institute of Aeronautics and Astronautics
  (1970), the John Adam Fleming Medal by the American Geophysical
  Union (1972), when he was just 39, the Tsiolkovsky Medal by the
  Kosmonautical Federation of the USSR (1987) the Chapman Medal by
  the Royal Astronomical Society (1994), and the New Zealand
  Science and Technology Gold Medal (1994).
  Sir Ian was a member of the Academies of Sciences of the US and
  Europe, and of the Royal Societies of London and of New Zealand,
  and a Fellow of the American Geophysical Union. For three years
  from 1982, he was Vice-Chancellor at Victoria University in
  Wellington, where he led the creation of the research school of
  earth sciences and encouraged the development of the Institute
  of Policy Studies and the Stout Research Centre.
  Current Vice-Chancellor Professor Pat Walsh said today Sir Ian
  was a strong supporter of the arts, particularly music, and
  championed improvements to the Kelburn campus, including the
  creation of the university marae.
  In recent years Sir Ian took an interest in global warming,
  arguing that wider use of nuclear energy would be better for the
  planet than countries such as China, Australia and the USA
  burning "all the fossil fuel they can lay their hands on, which
  would double or triple the amount of anthropogenic CO2 in the
  atmosphere".
  Convinced that life exists elsewhere in the universe - which he
  said was the normal view in astrophysics - Sir Ian was also an
  advocate for New Zealand participation in the world's biggest
  radio telescope project, the Square
  Kilometre Array project.

Flatwoods Sandbagged By MonsterQuest




                                                          UFO Updates
                                   A mailing list for the study of UFO-related phenomena
                                               'Its All Here In Black & White'
    Location: UFOUpDatesList.Com > 2010 > Mar > Mar 17

                                            Flatwoods Sandbagged By MonsterQuest
  From: Alfred Lehmberg <alienview.nul>
  Date: Wed, 17 Mar 2010 10:48:40 -0500
  Archived: Wed, 17 Mar 2010 12:29:23 -0400
  Subject: Flatwoods Sandbagged By MonsterQuest

  Folks, regarding the recent History Channel MonsterQuest episode
  of March 10th featuring Fred May, Frank Feschino, Stanton
  Friedman, and other witnesses from the town of Flatwoods, West
  Virginia: I was the bearded fellow, the only one, I think,
  associated with the Flatwoods segment exclusively. I wore the
  UFO Magazine hat. I was working with the Helium Balloon and
  assisting Feschino vis a vis the sighting at the hunter's camp
  in deep forest beside the spring fed stream. My one spoken line,
  used apart from where I actually said it was, "Frank, there's a
  hot spot up there..." or some such...
  All that said:
  Folks? You can quote me!
  I have no idea what that program was about! Why, apart from a
  true to form Joe Nickell, I didn't even recognize who was
  involved in it!
  This is _real_ irony, reader, given I was at Flatwoods for a
  week during the shooting -and I do mean shooting - of the MQ
  program. Moreover, I have an appropriate intimacy with all the
  principals shown on the Flatwoods segment and have better than
  a layman's understanding of just what occurred in and around
  Flatwoods that Indian summer night in 1952.
  Ladies and Gentleman, let me digress to say that, entirely apart
  from what the Reader saw on a flawed MonsterQuest, _this_ is
  what occurred on that one night in Flatwoods in Flatwoods:
  http://paratopiary.blogspot.com/
  I remind the _honest_ reader that this referenced map data is
  supported by Project Bluebook, named newspaper reportage, and
  first person witnesses in that order of numeracy.
  The History Channel, one finds, had the time, opportunity, and
  all the requisite data to produce a stunning program about the
  infamous Flatwoods affair. What the History channel did instead,
  reader, was to contrive to manufacture a senseless 'mash-up' of
  two entirely unrelated cases from what could be most easily be
  'faux-discredited' in either of them. Suggesting this bogus
  relationship, one not even remotely tenuous, is the program's
  kiss of less-than-mediocre death.
  Sincerely, none but those entirely honest with themselves dare
  call this very poor, contrived, and inauspicious telling of the
  Flatwoods story a blithering incompetence, a fatuous
  cluelessness, or a distorted propaganda! More irony is revealed
  given Feschino, Friedman, and I had to sign sworn statements
  indicating our contribution to the program was true as we knew
  it to be true. The History Channel reportage of same,
  paradoxically, was not.
  See? Flatwoods was the tail end of the biggest UFO Flap in US
  History: The 1952 Summer Of Saucers, chronicled by Frank
  Feschino, Wendy Connors, various other authors and an un-sifted
  Project Bluebook. Reader! It was _not_ about "Lizard Monsters"
  allegedly lurking the woods for 60 plus years and to this day.


http://www.ufoupdateslist.com/2010/mar/m17-006.shtml[10/12/2011 14:19:46]
Flatwoods Sandbagged By MonsterQuest

  This is the distortion prosecuted by the History Channel.
  And this! The intrepid MonsterQuest documentarians wrongly
  called the more honest Stanton Friedman a "Doctor" and made the
  dissembling (to be kind) Dr. (degree immaterial) Nickell look
  'reasonable' in contrived comparison! Glowing eyes? Not before
  or since. Ground miasma? Not before or since! Mass hysteria? Not
  before or since! Noxious weeds? Not before or since! Roc sized
  barn owls? Not before or since! How could they have got things
  so canted and wrong!
  I'm sick at heart and really ticked off... Feschino, who
  deserves better than this, was fit to be tied. See, he's telling
  the culture changing real story. Nickell and company shill for
  the guys insulting the viewer's intelligence and obscuring real
  history. Case in point "Mass Hysteria" as touted by Dr.
  Nickell... is a clueless dodge.
  Why? The witnesses at Flatwoods, a gang of playing children and
  a couple of young adults, presupposed a meteor, predominantly,
  on the Fisher farm in the hills above the school that evening.
  They'd heard about them recently in school. Nickell _dissembled_
  when he reported they expected "monsters"... They did not run up
  a hill armed with only with a flashlight to look for "monsters",
  Reader! That only happens in the movies and Joe Nickell's facile
  imagination! They went up the hill to pick up pieces of a
  meteorite!
  No, the Flatwoods story was not remotely told. The historical

  Over the past year or so I got back in contact with lots of old
  colleagues of mine in the mainstream news biz. Many are legends
  in the industry with tons of kudos and even though I broke from
  the fold years ago they still treated me with respect and
  friendship. They know I do UFO research and love the idea
  although some can't step into that arena. I now know why and
  it's what I suspected way back then.
  We all know the news industry has done a horrid job of reporting
  UFO incidents. I've stated time and time again on this list, on
  radio and elsewhere to_never_trust_mainstream_news regarding
  UFOs. I'd chalk it all up to incompetence but that would be
  incompetent of me to do so. It's just outright hostility toward
  the UFO subject supported by intelligence community agents and
  their associates.
  This tv special as you Alfred have pointed out had more than
  enough time and resources to do a good job but didn't. Why?
  It may be they did until it got to the producer and editor's
  hands.
  If it's just a case of sloppiness, or inattentiveness it makes
  you wonder how accurate their other projects are.
  You guys in mainstream ufology have to make an honest stand from
  hereon out and boycott the news and documentary groups that have
  shown to do a half_ass job. Don't thirst for the attention they
  appear to want to languish on you so you can get your point
  across, just stand up and say and make a public statement you
  don't respect their unprofessional work on past projects.
  You don't need mainstream news anymore. Just look at the death
  toll of the newsroom workforce. I've got several dozen pro level
  colleagues who've done 30 years or more coming to me asking me
  about how to blog. They got the boot from newsrooms and now are
  chewing rawhide to keep their heads above water. It's horrible
  to see. I left mainstream news because of the influx and strong
  arm tactics of the gangsters and military industrial
  knuckleheads that were pushing their agendas. They will twist
  the news into anyway they can to manipulate the public and it
  worked, until the internet expanded.
  You don't like the documentaries and news stories? Then make
  your own documentaries, however in the new works, let the public
  know why you're making your own because the public deserves the
  facts not the manipulation.
  UFOs are the toughest subject to breach. You veterans of this
  research know it. It takes more than guts to approach it and
  more than guts to report it. You have to deal with stonewalling,
  insults, attacks, ostracizing, budgets, hostility, threats and
  more.
  I've said it before, if there wasn't something there, we
  wouldn't run into so much resistance. Usually what's causing the
  resistance is the manifestation of the fears of those who have
  committed crimes in that area. Somebody is withholding a big ass
  FUBAR, one that reaches the stars themselves.

  From: Jerome Clark <jkclark.nul>
  Date: Wed, 17 Mar 2010 13:46:57 -0400

  Subject: Re: Flatwoods Sandbagged By MonsterQuest

  >>Date: Wed, 17 Mar 2010 10:48:40 -0500
  >>Subject: Flatwoods Sandbagged By MonsterQuest
  >Mr. Lehmberg, I despise MonsterQuest.
  >Below is my eloquently titled blog entry on why MonsterQuest
  >needs to be canceled.
  >http://tinyurl.com/yflvg7g
  MonsterQuest is the only anomalies-themed series I watch on
  television. I rarely miss it, in fact.
  Consequently, I know its strengths and its weaknesses fairly
  well. In my judgment the former outweigh the latter. MQ's
  strength is its ability to bring in - i.e., afford - outside
  experts to analyze physical evidence, films, and videos and to
  provide broader zoological contexts. With one exception that
  comes to mind (a mind-numbingly awful episode some months ago
  about an alleged alien corpse recovered in Mexico [_not_ Lloyd
  Pye's Starchild, on which I have no opinion whatever]), the
  reporting seems earnest and straightforward. At its worst, the
  show is just sort of dumb and pointless, but that's true of
  pretty much any TV series on any subject.
  On the positive side of the ledger, I learn of reported cryptids
  with which I am sometimes unfamiliar. It's interesting to see
  the witnesses speaking directly, as opposed to through the
  printed page or computer screen. I enjoy the on-site
  reconstructions which give me a clearer mental picture of what
  has been reported.
  It doesn't bother me that MQ deals from time to time with animal
  matters that are only vaguely cryptozoological. After all, there
  is not a bottomless supply of alleged cryptids to draw from.
  The core problem with the show is that only a relative few
  cryptids are more than shadowy entities (in the metaphorical,
  not literal, sense). Reports arise, in other words, from
  experience anomalies, not anomalous events, and thus documenting
  unknown ostensible animals' presence from anything but sincere
  testimony is probably hopeless. Nonetheless, the effort is worth
  making, even if its ultimate effect is to generate skepticism of
  the literalist interpretation of cryptid encounters.
  Unfortunately, after four years MQ's formula is wearing thin,
  which I imagine is why the show is rumored to be on the chopping
  block. By now, viewers have figured out that real proof of a
  really interesting this-world cryptid will not be forthcoming.
  Most people have no tolerance for ambiguity, and that's all MQ
  gets for being honest about its subject.

  Hi All,
  Just as an aside, has anyone ever heard of or has it ever been
  documented that a circle maker has employed either microwave
  channeled or heated-exhaust based devices with spread apertures
  to create crop formations? Any evidence of anyone ever using
  such technology or creating such or similar devices? I notice
  quite often in crop circle photos from the air that you can
  often (usually) discern roughly 3 to 5 foot wide rings of
  impressions within each or most of the larger parts of various
  formations over time that have been made, which would be a
  signature of basic man-made crop circles, although I think there
  may also have been in the past, or perhaps very rarely during
  current times formations of simple circles that might be made by
  natural atmospheric plasma formations or small whirlwinds in
  crops, but I would assume none of those have the kind of precise
  edges or certainly not complex formations most often observed.
  Just wondering what kind of tech could be employed to create

Re: The Biggest Crop Circle Ever: The Reality Show

  crop circles or other kinds of formations in natural media might
  be technically possible that would be obviously different in
  residual effects or patterns than the standard 'board-stomp"
  ones most commonly done.
  Didn't Vallee once posit the possibility of aerial platforms
  (such as helicopters or aircraft) or even satellite-based
  orbiting platforms employing microwave laser or "maser"
  technology, with some idea that patterns could be invisibly cut
  into fields in a manner which would not display "board-stomp"
  effects, and which with computer controlled or programmed
  focusing tech, could create elaborate formations very quickly
  without anyone being present in the fields themselves? Any
  validity or evidence for his speculations?

  Alfred, I take your objections seriously, and I largely concur
  with your specific complaints. Please note, however, that in my
  remarks I did not mention the Flatwoods episode specifically. My
  post was directed generally to MQ which, for what it is (as
  opposed to a Platonic ideal), is fairly well regarded by
  cryptozoologists of my acquaintance.
  Where the Flatwoods show is concerned, the problem is that it's
  a CE3, not an encounter with an unknown animal or creature --
  ufology, in other words, not cryptozoology. MQ did a clumsy to
  lousy job of trying to cram a square peg into a round hole.
  Knowing what it was doing and being cynical about television
  shows on anomalies as a broad principle, I didn't take that
  quite so personally as you - understandably, being a
  participant - did.

  Cordially,
  Jerry Clark

  Thank you, you have elevated the discussion to the appropriate level.
  It's hardly an aside. I have found a number of other discussions
  relevant to this issue that might interest list readers.
  An online book entitled "UFOs: The Military Unmasked" by Emmanuel
  Dehlinger was translated from French and may be found at
  http://ovnis-armee.org/ufos_military_unmasked.htm
  The book includes a chapter entitled "Crop Circles" that makes a case
  that microwaves were used, probably by the military, in the creation
  of crop formations.
  http://ovnis-armee.org/5_crop_circles.htm
  People have reported balls of light roaming the fields around
  where and when formations have appeared, and some have
  speculated that these orbs have something to do with the
  creation of the formations. The crop circle connector website
  has a number of essays relavant to this issue.
  The essay An Hypothesis: The Transmission Of A Crop Circle is
  an interesting analysis of the possible dynamics of something
  like a plasma ball creating a crop formation.
  http://www.cropcircleconnector.com/ilyes/ilyes.html
  http://www.cropcircleconnector.com/ilyes/Intro1.html
  Consistent with this is Czech Witnesses' Field Experience
  Supports BoL-generation of Formations published at
  http://www.cropcircleconnector.com/ilyes/ilyes17.html
  It includes a discussion of the ball-of-light dynamics seen on
  video.
  Then there is More On Those Magical Balls Of Light: Eyewitness
  In Holland Watches Circles Manifest" at
  http://www.cropcircleconnector.com/ilyes/ilyes20.html
  A farmer in Poland describes his experience, including physical
  effects, while watching a nearby strange object.
  http://tinyurl.com/ykh7wxu
  In this case, he saw a red spherical light nearby that "appeared
  to be some kind of craft". The next day, a neighbor told him of
  a complex pattern in the field consisting of several connected
  crop circles.
  In general, there seems to be some kind of high technology in
  use. The effects seem consistent with microwave irradiation, but
  how this would be delivered is still very much a mystery. It's
  possible that the technology is terrestrial in origin. If so, it
  is very sophisticated and likely not in the public domain.
  Aftereffects appear to linger and affect people and instruments
  entering the formations. And, yes, UFOs seem to be involved at
  times.

                                           PRG Update - 3/20/10 - X-Conference 2010
  From: Paradigm Research Group <PRG.nul>
  Date: Sat, 20 Mar 2010 15:54:42 -0700
  Archived: Sun, 21 Mar 2010 07:57:07 -0400
  Subject: PRG Update - 3/20/10 - X-Conference 2010

  PRG
  Paradigm Research Group
  PRG Update - March 20, 2010
  X-Conference 2010
  [Note: PRG is about to enter a very intense period and
  there will be a series of updates sent out in the coming
  days and weeks.]
  X-Conference 2010 - May 7-9
  National Press Club
  529 14th Street, NW, Washington, DC
  www.x-conference.com
  PRG's sixth X-Conference will be held at the National Press Club
  two blocks from the White House, twelve blocks from the Capitol
  and near every major national and international media
  organization covering the U. S. government. It was scheduled to
  coincide with the 9th anniversary of the May 9, 2001 witness
  press conference conducted by the Disclosure Project in the same
  ballroom.
  While there have been numerous press events at the NPC
  addressing UFO/ET related issues, this will be the first time a
  full conference on this subject matter has been held there. The
  NPC will be the permanent home for the X-Conferences which will
  be held on the weekend nearest the May 9, 2001 anniversary.
  The close proximity to the White House, Congress and media
  (there are scores of news organizations based in the National
  Press Building) increases the prospect of political staffers and
  press attending the conference itself over the weekend. As
  always, invites will go out to White House and Congressional
  staff. A strong turnout will send a message the issue is not
  going away and the present administration must engage it. If you
  have been thinking of attending in the past, this is the year to
  come.
  The National Press Club is two blocks from the Metro Center
  Station. There are two dozen hotels close by. PRG has a discount
  arrangement with the Harrington and J. W. Marriott hotels.
  This year there will be 8 lectures, 2 special luncheon
  presentations, a Speaker's Cocktail Party and the PRG Awards
  Banquet. A press conference will be held on Monday, May 10 at 10
  am in the NPC's First Amendment Room.
  Former CNN news anchor Cheryll Jones will host. Speakers will
  include: George Noory of Coast to Coast AM (Special Luncheon
  Presentation); George Haas and William Saunders (Special
  Luncheon Presentation); Stephen Bassett; Richard Dolan; A. J.
  Gevaerd (Brazil); Gary Heseltine (UK); Linda Moulton Howe;
  George Knapp; Paul Stonehill and Jim Nichols.
  Topics will include: the government imposed truth embargo; the
  Rockefeller Initiative and the UFO/ET connections to the Obama
  administration; the Cydonia/Martian Codex; Disclosure; the RAF
  Bentwaters case; the Eisenhower briefing memo; UFOs and the


http://www.ufoupdateslist.com/2010/mar/m21-001.shtml[10/12/2011 14:19:52]
PRG Update - 3/20/10 - X-Conference 2010

  National Security State; police sightings database and
  developments in the UK; extraordinary developments in Brazil;
  UFO/ET connections to Russia and the Soviet Union; more.
  Full information and online registration at:
  www.x-conference.com

  2010 PRG Awards
  PRG generally does not announce awards in advance but does make
  an exception with regards to posthumous recipients. The 2010
  posthumous PRG Award for Lifetime Achievement (Hall of Fame)
  will go to Richard Hall.

  UFO Digest Mentoring Post Graduate Student With Phd.
  Dissertation In Ufology

  http://www.ufodigest.com/news/0310/hoyt.php

  Robert Morningstar asks Ufologists, contactees and ETAP
  experiencers to help Diana Hoyt (Virginia Tech grad student)
  with her Post Graduate UFO Dissertation Survey on UFOlogy.
  Diana Hoyt is going to work for NASA and I think it is in
  everyone's interest to have more people (knowledgeable about
  UFOs) with some input there.
  Please help Diana in gathering data for her dissertation by
  answering her survey and sharing it with your email lists.
  I ask those of you with websites to post the link below,
  directing your readers to the survey. The survey link is on Page
  3 of the article.
  The survey takes from 15 minutes to half an hour to fill out,
  depending on the degree of detail that you wish to share. You
  may submit your replies anonymously or with your name and email
  address, if you so choose.

  Thank you,
  Robert

                                                        Secrecy News -- 03/22/10
  From: Steven Aftergood <saftergood.nul>
  Date: Mon, 22 Mar 2010 13:59:16 -0400
  Archived: Mon, 22 Mar 2010 17:29:54 -0400
  Subject: Secrecy News -- 03/22/10

  SECRECY NEWS
  from the FAS Project on Government Secrecy
  Volume 2010, Issue No. 23
  March 22, 2010
  Secrecy News Blog: http://www.fas.org/blog/secrecy/

  **JUDGE: IF YOU LEAK CLASSIFIED INFO, TAKE THE CONSEQUENCES
  **THE FIRST AMENDMENT AND "NEWSGATHERING CRIMES"
  **BOOK: THE IRAQ PAPERS
  **HOMELAND SECURITY INTELLIGENCE

  JUDGE: IF YOU LEAK CLASSIFIED INFO, TAKE THE CONSEQUENCES
  Leaking classified information to an unauthorized person may be
  the right thing to do in certain circumstances, suggested Judge
  T.S. Ellis, III of the Eastern District of Virginia in a newly
  released hearing transcript from last year. In particular, he
  said, leaking may be an acceptable move if the leaker accepts
  full responsibility for his actions.
  Ordinarily, disclosing classified information to an unauthorized
  person is deemed unethical, if one has signed a non-disclosure
  agreement not to do so. It may also be illegal, if the
  classified information falls within certain categories whose
  unauthorized disclosure is proscribed by law (including
  communications intelligence-related information, identities of
  covert agents, nuclear weapons design information, and "national
  defense information").
  "Whistleblowing" in itself is not an adequate rationale for
  leaking classified information, Judge Ellis said. "Simply
  because you believe that something that's going on that's
  classified should be revealed to the press and to the public, so
  that the public can know that its government is doing something
  you think is wrong, that doesn't justify [publicly disclosing]
  it.... Noble motives don't erase the violation."
  However, he said, "you may want to go ahead and do it [anyway],
  but you have to stand up and take the consequences."
  "I don't have a problem with people doing that if they are held
  accountable for it," Judge Ellis said. "One might hope that, for
  example, someone might have the courage to do something that
  would break the law if it meant they're the savior of the
  country. But then one has to take the consequences, because the
  rule of law is so important."
  "Disclosing it was okay if the person is willing to stand up and
  say, 'I did it. Give me the consequences'."
  Judge Ellis spoke at a June 11, 2009 hearing on the reduction of
  the sentence for Lawrence A. Franklin, who was convicted of
  disclosing classified information to two officials of the
  American Israel Public Affairs Committee, Steven Rosen and Keith
  Weissman, who were themselves charged with unauthorized receipt
  and disclosure of classified information. The controversial case


http://www.ufoupdateslist.com/2010/mar/m22-004.shtml[10/12/2011 14:19:54]
Secrecy News -- 03/22/10

  against them was abandoned by the government last year. Based on
  his cooperation with prosecutors, Mr. Franklin sought and
  received a reduction in his own 12-year prison sentence to
  probation and ten months in "community confinement."
  In addition, Mr. Franklin was ordered to spend 100 hours "giving
  talks to young people" about the need to protect classified
  information. "What I want you to speak to these young people
  about, Mr. Franklin, is the rule of law and the obligation that
  public officials have and the importance of classified
  information," Judge Ellis explained. "Secrets are important to a
  nation. If we couldn't keep our secrets, we would be at greater
  risk.... And I am going to ask that a probation officer send me
  copies of your lectures on this subject."
  Mr. Franklin is the second person convicted of unauthorized
  disclosure of classified information in a non-espionage case.
  The first was Samuel L. Morison, who was convicted of providing
  classified intelligence photographs to Jane's Defence Weekly in
  1985. The third conviction, still pending, is that of Shamai
  Leibowitz, a former FBI translator who pled guilty last December
  to the unauthorized disclosure of five classified documents to a
  blogger. Prosecutors specifically acknowledged Mr. Leibowitz's
  "affirmative acceptance of personal responsibility for his
  criminal conduct."
  The transcript of Mr. Franklin's June 2009 sentence hearing was
  finally prepared last week. A copy was obtained by Secrecy News
  and posted on the website of the Federation of American
  Scientists.
    http://www.fas.org/sgp/jud/aipac/franklin061109.html

  THE FIRST AMENDMENT AND "NEWSGATHERING CRIMES"
  It might be pleasant for writers and publishers to suppose that
  First Amendment principles of freedom of speech and freedom of
  the press are absolute and will prevail in every circumstance.
  But that is clearly not the case.
  For one thing, the Supreme Court has specifically excluded
  obscenity, child pornography, and certain other forms of
  communication from First Amendment protections. See "Freedom of
  Speech and Press: Exceptions to the First Amendment,"
  Congressional Research Service, updated October 16, 2009:
    http://www.fas.org/sgp/crs/misc/95-815.pdf
  Moreover, courts have repeatedly upheld the constitutionality of
  prohibitions in the Espionage Act against the unauthorized
  disclosure of certain types of classified information (most
  recently in a 2006 ruling in the AIPAC case, USA v. Rosen and
  Weissman).
  The intersection of national security law and ordinary
  newsgathering remains a bit murky, and is contested in some
  quarters even where it is fairly clear. Although "the right of
  the press to publish confidential information is well
  established, [t]here is... a paucity of constitutional doctrine
  protecting newsgathering activities that seek the leaking of
  confidential information," according to a recent law review
  article.
  "Ethics codes for news organizations state that reporters must
  not commit crimes such as trespassing or stealing information
  but are silent on inchoate crimes such as solicitation," wrote
  Prof. William E. Lee of the University of Georgia last year.
  "And while news organizations have elaborate rules about
  relations with confidential sources, they do not address the
  propriety of promising confidentiality as an inducement to the
  disclosure of classified information."
  "Although there are practical and political difficulties in
  prosecuting reporters for solicitation or conspiracy, there is
  little First Amendment precedent in support of the argument that
  reporters should be exempt from generally applicable criminal
  laws."See "Probing Secrets: The Press and Inchoate Liability for
  Newsgathering Crimes" by William E. Lee, American Journal of
  Criminal Law, vol. 36, no. 2, Spring 2009 (not online).



http://www.ufoupdateslist.com/2010/mar/m22-004.shtml[10/12/2011 14:19:54]
Secrecy News -- 03/22/10

  The longstanding conflict over press publication of national
  security information is revisited in the forthcoming book
  "Necessary Secrets" by Gabriel Schoenfeld (Norton Books, May
  2010).
  http://books.wwnorton.com/books/Necessary-Secrets/

  BOOK: THE IRAQ PAPERS
  An extensive compilation of official documents, policy advocacy
  statements, and assorted commentary on the U.S. decision to go
  to war in Iraq in 2003 is presented in "The Iraq Papers," a new
  book from Oxford University Press.
  Since it seems that there will be no new official reckoning of
  the Iraq war or other Bush Administration policy choices, it
  will be left to others to achieve their own understanding of the
  Bush era and its aftermath. "The Iraq Papers" provides one
  possible documentary starting point.
  "The decision to invade Iraq launched a new doctrine of
  preemptive war, mired the American military in an intractable
  armed conflict, disrupted world petroleum supplies, cost the
  United States billions of dollars, and damaged or ended the
  lives of hundreds of thousands of Americans and Iraqis," the
  book states.
  The book editors are not overly perplexed by these events.
  Somewhat heavy-handedly, they offer their own interpretation of
  events involving the decisive influence of neo-conservatives,
  the unitary executive, and a U.S. drive to global hegemony,
  among other factors. Alternative explanations are not considered
  here.
  See "The Iraq Papers," edited by John Ehrenberg, J. Patrice
  McSherry, Jose Ramon Sanchez, and Caroleen Marji Sayej, Oxford
  University Press, January 2010:
  http://www.amazon.com/Iraq-Papers-John-Ehrenberg/dp/0195398599

  HOMELAND SECURITY INTELLIGENCE
  An updated description of the intelligence function of the
  Department of Homeland Security was produced last week by the
  Congressional Research Service. See "The Department of Homeland
  Security Intelligence Enterprise: Operational Overview and
  Oversight Challenges for Congress," March 19, 2010:
  http://www.fas.org/sgp/crs/homesec/R40602.pdf
  "Homeland Security Intelligence: Its Relevance and Limitations"
  was the topic of a March 18, 2009 hearing of the House Homeland
  Security Committee, the record of which was published last
  month.
  http://www.fas.org/irp/congress/2009_hr/hsin.html

  From: Eleanor White <ewraven1.nul>
  Date: Tue, 23 Mar 2010 17:00:31 -0400
  Archived: Tue, 23 Mar 2010 17:26:47 -0400
  Subject: Re: Boomerang UFO?

  >From: Lyle Michel <ljm1915.nul>
  >To: <post.nul>
  >Date: Tue, 23 Mar 2010 12:45:35 -0700
  >Subject: Boomerang UFO?
  <snip>
  >I would like to suggest that our government is likely using
  >antigravitational craft to put satellites in orbit. I don't hear
  >or read about many rocket launches of satellites and yet we have
  >hundreds if not thousands in orbit. How did they all get up
  >there?
  >Lyle Michel
  I've wondered from time to time about something.
  Fairly early in the U.S. space program, we would often hear of
  military satellite launches from the _west_ coast, i.e.
  Vandenburg Air Force Base.
  As best I understand the reason for the East coast location of
  Cape Kennedy/Canaveral was that they could launch _with_ the
  rotation of the Earth, assisting in reaching orbital velocity.
  _And_ the Atlantic Ocean was a safe place for failed launches to
  crash in.
  Failed Vandenburg launches would crash into the U.S., not an
  ideal situation, it would seem.
  I wondered why the safe crash zone idea wasn't considered
  important for the military launches. A tie to anti-grav
  technology is way too tenuous to be made seriously, but I still
  wonder why the military satellite launches were made on the West
  coast, with the seeming hazard of crashes into the U.S.

  Eleanor White

  AZTEC UFO 2010 SYMPOSIUM
  UFOs - Then & Now
  March 26-27, 2010 in Aztec, New Mexico
  The UFO Symposium is a yearly UFO conference held in Aztec, New
  Mexico where there was reported to be a UFO crash in March 1948
  just 9 months after the Roswell, New Mexico incident.
  The yearly Aztec ufo conference objective is to bring
  researchers in to discuss UFOs - Dulce, New Mexico, Exopolitics,
  alien contactees , remote viewing - sightings - alien abductions
  and encounters - government cover-ups - New Mexico UFOs -
  pictures and videos of ufos - crop circles - cattle mutilations
  - underground bases - and more.
  This year we will focus on various topics that allude to the
  idea that Aliens and UFOs have visited earth in the past and are
  visiting here now.
  Our Speakers for March 26-27, 2010 will be:
  Art Campbell - Researcher - "Did Eisenhower meet the ETs.at
  Holloman AFB in 1955?"
  Paola Harris - Researcher - "Exopolitics: Et Phone home..."
  Stan Romanek - Experiencer - "The Stan Romanek case is possibly
  the most scientifically documented human extraterrestrial
  encounters case in the world!"
  Angelia Joiner - Researcher/Reporter - "Stephenville, Then and
  Now"
  Kenn Thomas - Writer/Publisher - "Wilhelm Reich, Eisenhower and
  the Aliens"
  Lyn Buchanan - Remote Viewer - "Results from the remote viewing
  visit of the 1948 Aztec crash site."
  NEWS FLASH!! - Stanton Friedman will be at the event and
  enlighten us about his new research and upcoming book "Science
  Was Wrong"

Willingham And The Del Rio Crash




                                                          UFO Updates
                                   A mailing list for the study of UFO-related phenomena
                                               'Its All Here In Black & White'
    Location: UFOUpDatesList.Com > 2010 > Mar > Mar 25

                                                Willingham And The Del Rio Crash
  From: Kevin Randle <KRandle993.nul>
  Date: Wed, 24 Mar 2010 14:41:07 EDT
  Archived: Thu, 25 Mar 2010 18:52:46 -0400
  Subject: Willingham And The Del Rio Crash

  Hello, All
  As you know, Robert B. Willingham, in an appearance on Jeff
  Rense's show, made a lot of claims, including that he was a
  retired military officer, a colonel, had been a fighter pilot
  and was a veteran of both WW II and the Korean War. He said he
  saw a flying saucer crash and talked about events in the mid-
  1950s because he couldn't remember the exact date.
  For the last year, I have attempted to verify the military
  service of this guy. His 1977 affidavit was believed by many
  because he said he was a retired Air Force colonel. He talked of
  his experiences after he had seen a UFO crash in northern
  Mexico, some suggesting Del Rio and he, lately suggesting a
  place south of Laredo. He talked of having been denied a pension
  because he talked about the UFO crash. He has been interviewed
  on the radio... and told Jeff Rense that he had been to seven
  crash sites, described what he had seen and done, and was
  believed because he was a retired colonel.
  I checked the various sources and could find no corroboration
  for this. The NARA records center in St. Louis provided a
  document that suggested he had been in the Army from December
  1945 to January 1947, that he had risen to E4, a very low
  enlisted rank and that is it. Nothing else to prove his
  service... of course he claims that his records were destroyed
  in the fire there in 1973. Except, of course, if we believe his
  story, his records wouldn't have been there in 1973 because he
  had yet to retire.
  Here are the facts, as I have been able to determine them.
  Willingham, after his military service, joined the Civil Air
  Patrol, a volunteer organization that is a civilian auxiliary of
  the Air Force. Membership in it is not the same as membership in
  the Air Force Reserve. Willingham was, as best as I can
  determine, eventually a lieutenant colonel in that. He has tried
  to convert his membership in the CAP into that of the Air Force
  Reserve.
  I have a document he submitted to the records center that lists
  his "Air Force" service and one of the entries on that document
  show that from 1964 to 1966 he claims Air Force reserve service
  at the Mechanicsburg Navy Base, Squadron 308. Another document
  shows that Squadron 308 is a Civil Air Patrol unit.
  That same document lists his "A.F." C.A.P. serial number, but
  there is no such thing. This is his CAP serial number and
  nothing else.
  I have a copy of Form 249-3, which is supposed to have come from
  the records center, but it is clear that he has made many
  alterations to it. There are misspellings on it, entries that
  would not appear on a real form, and a couple of mistakes such
  as claiming more than 400 retirement points in a period of less
  than one year... the max is 365 for an entire year.
  Yes, there was a fire in the records center and many veteran
  records were destroyed, but there are other sources of the
  information. He has no DD 214 form filed at the Air Reserve


http://www.ufoupdateslist.com/2010/mar/m25-001.shtml[10/12/2011 14:19:58]
Willingham And The Del Rio Crash

  Personnel Center in Denver, there are no records at the various
  training bases in San Antonio where he would have learned to
  fly, there are no VA records anywhere to show he was injured in
  combat related events. His name appears on no rosters where it
  should and while I freely admit that my records, when I
  attempted to retrieve them as an experiment were horribly
  inaccurate, there were records and they did confirm some of my
  service in the Air Force. The difference is that Willingham has
  found nothing.
  For those who have said that Willingham's tale is a free speech
  issue, unless he tried to get a pension or something, let me say
  this. He claims he has attempted, for many years to get a
  pension. The documentation proves that... and it proves he
  didn't, and doesn't deserve one. So, in this case it's really
  not a free speech issue but an attempt to gain a pension... Not
  to mention his distortion of the UFO sighting and claims of a
  crash in northern Mexico.
  Finally, I found, in Skylook from February/March of 1968, the
  original story of the UFO crash he claimed to have witnessed.
  According to that, he was flying an aircraft that did not exist
  in 1948, the original date of the crash... yes, he originally
  said 1948, it evolved into 1950 and now is sometime in the mid-
  1950s. That's not the only problem with the tale. He talked of
  seeing three UFOs and that he had been prevented from making an
  investigation by the Mexicans and that he was never any closer
  than 60 feet.
  Please note the expansion of his story, which is the mark of a
  hoax... yes, I know that some people are reluctant to talk at
  first and as they become comfortable with the researcher will
  add details. And there is something to be said for the
  questioning opening up details as well, but here we have a guy
  who saw something in the distance, then he was right up close,
  then he took a piece, then he was working with someone at the
  Pentagon and was involved in seven retrievals. And, we can't
  find anything to corroborate his alleged military career and
  without that, his stories collapse.
  There is more stuff... the only pictures of Willingham I have
  seen are of him in a CAP uniform with the lapel insignia removed
  so that it isn't as easily identifiable as CAP. However, he is
  wearing CAP ribbons on it and that can't be done on an Air Force
  uniform.
  But why continue?. This story is bogus and unless Willingham can
  present some documents that can be verified, we must now reject
  his tale.

  KRandle

  From: William Sawers <ufsyntax.nul>
  Date: Fri, 26 Mar 2010 09:35:54 +1000
  Archived: Fri, 26 Mar 2010 07:51:22 -0400
  Subject: UFO Buzzes Sydney

  List,
  A woman saw several UFOs around sunset on 24/03/10 in Sydney.

                                            Re: Willingham And The Del Rio Crash
  From: Greg Boone <Evolbaby.nul>
  Date: Fri, 26 Mar 2010 09:49:45 EDT
  Archived: Fri, 26 Mar 2010 10:02:11 -0400
  Subject: Re: Willingham And The Del Rio Crash

  >From: Kevin Randle <KRandle993.nul>
  >To: post.nul
  >Date: Wed, 24 Mar 2010 14:41:07 EDT
  >Subject: Willingham And The Del Rio Crash
  >As you know, Robert B. Willingham, in an appearance on Jeff
  >Rense's show, made a lot of claims, including that he was a
  >retired military officer, a colonel, had been a fighter pilot
  >and was a veteran of both WW II and the Korean War. He said he
  >saw a flying saucer crash and talked about events in the mid-
  >1950s because he couldn't remember the exact date.
  >For the last year, I have attempted to verify the military
  >service of this guy. His 1977 affidavit was believed by many
  >because he said he was a retired Air Force colonel. He talked of
  >his experiences after he had seen a UFO crash in northern
  >Mexico, some suggesting Del Rio and he, lately suggesting a
  >place south of Laredo. He talked of having been denied a pension
  >because he talked about the UFO crash. He has been interviewed
  >on the radio... and told Jeff Rense that he had been to seven
  >crash sites, described what he had seen and done, and was
  >believed because he was a retired colonel.
  <snip>
  >But why continue? This story is bogus and unless Willingham can
  >present some documents that can be verified, we must now reject
  >his tale.

  Yes, Willingham's exploits need documentation for validation in
  a big way. Bits and pieces of things appear and I'm still
  waiting for his authors to respond.
  However, because his story went from being on a UFO crash
  retrieval team to being best friends with late President Lyndon
  Johnson, to being a personal aide to the late General George S.
  Patton, I was waiting for him to start spinning a yarn about how
  he saved the world during the Cuban Missile Crisis by hitting
  Fidel Castro in the back of the head with a pea shooter while
  hanging from a top secret invisibility dirigible he personally
  designed along with his best pal Albert Einstein.
  No documentation, no story. When he forks something over we'll
  take the next step. I'm not pursuing it, don't have the time.
  All I can say is what I have dug up is interesting in other
  areas of Ufology that I have been long meaning to address and
  will soon but that's something way off course here.
  Paperwork, witnesses, validation. That's what Willingham needs.

  Best,
  Greg
  ufomafia.com

  of our unit was to launch what was called the Defense
  Meteorological Satellite Program (DMSP) bird into polar orbit.
  This program was very highly classified back then.
  We also launched several missiles from Johnston Atoll in the
  Pacific down range into the Kawajelin Atoll as part of the
  Intermediate Range Ballistic Missile Defense Program.

                                                            Could It Be A UFO?
  From: Sheryl Gottschall <gottscha.nul>
  Date: Sat, 27 Mar 2010 18:09:07 +1000
  Archived: Sat, 27 Mar 2010 08:22:50 -0400
  Subject: Could It Be A UFO?

  Source: Campbelltown Macarthur News, Sydney, Australia
  http://tinyurl.com/yjwfrq3
  25 Mar, 2010
  Could it be a UFO?
  (with pic)
  Eagle Vale resident Shawaan Peck took this photo on an early
  Winter morning last year.
  He took the photo on his mobile phone and rushed inside to grab
  his handycam but claimed when he returned the "glowing
  objects" in the sky had disappeared.
  This week, there have been reports of UFO sightings in Chipping
  Norton and Mr Peck believed that was what he captured in this
  shot.
  "I've seen freaky things before, but they've been satellites or
  shooting stars," he told the Advertiser.
  "But this thing, I was like hello? what's that?
  "I thought it was just weird... I don't think we're alone."
  He said he included the street light in the shot to compare the
  objects with.

                                    Birmingham UFO Group's Larry Warren Lecture
  From: Philip Mantle <philip.nul>
  Date: Sun, 28 Mar 2010 10:23:52 +0100
  Archived: Sun, 28 Mar 2010 09:27:31 -0400
  Subject: Birmingham UFO Group's Larry Warren Lecture
  -----
  From: Dave Hodrien [mailto:davehodrien.nul]
  Sent: 27 March 2010 8:36 AM
  To: davehodrien.nul
  Subject: Birmingham UFO Group Larry Warren Lecture Advert
  Birmingham UFO Group Lecture
  Larry Warren
  Rendlesham: 30 Years Of Denial
  Date: Monday 12th April 2010
  Time: 7.30-11.30pm
  Location: Midland Red Social Club, Wolverhampton Road South,
  Birmingham, B322AY
  Price: Members =A34.00, Non-Members =A35.00
  (For further venue details check website at http://www.bufog.com)
  Larry Warren was the original whistleblower of the Rendlesham
  Forest incident one of the UK s most important UFO cases.
  Between 26 and 28th December 1980, numerous UFOs landed in close
  proximity to two Air Force bases, Woodbridge and Bentwaters.
  While many remained silent, Larry came forward with details
  about the stunning events which took place. Without his
  testimony, the incident may never have been brought to the
  public s attention.
  In this heart-felt talk, Larry will bring the case right up to
  date, cover latest developments, and explain why the case is
  still veiled in secrecy and disinformation. The lecture will
  include an overview of the case and Larry s involvement in it,
  and will feature a large amount of evidence to back up
  everything discussed. He will also cover various controversies
  surrounding the case and set the record straight. Over the
  years, he has gone through a lot, but despite this has continued
  to bravely fight to get the truth out.
  In his own words, This ain t no summer of love.
  Whether or not you have ever heard Larry speak before, this
  lecture is absolutely essential, and we are proud to welcome him
  to speak for Birmingham UFO Group. Hope to see you there!

KC Tanker & FB-111s 'UFOs'




                                                          UFO Updates
                                   A mailing list for the study of UFO-related phenomena
                                               'Its All Here In Black & White'
    Location: UFOUpDatesList.Com > 2010 > Mar > Mar 29

                                                    KC Tanker & FB-111s 'UFOs'
  From: Terry Colvin <fortean1.nul>
  Date: Mon, 29 Mar 2010 04:17:48 -0400 (EDT)
  Archived: Mon, 29 Mar 2010 07:58:16 -0400
  Subject: KC Tanker & FB-111s 'UFOs'

  ...This and similar events are possible sources of some UFO
  reports.
  Terry
  -----
  Sent: Sunday, March 28, 2010 7:51 PM
  To: U-Tapao.nul
  On 4/4/72, our KC (#63-8034) from 28ars (Ellsworth) was
  refueling 2 FB-111's on a clear night somewhere near Mountain
  Home - old log books are great. After we were done, one of the
  1-11 pilots came on the radio and asked "anyone on board besides
  the crew?" and I answered no... wondering why he was asking
  that.
  He then said to fly straight and level for a minute or so and
  they drifted off to each side of us and accelerated... when
  maybe a mile ahead of us, all of a sudden two huge fireballs
  erupted and went past us on each side of the tanker. Scared the
  hell out of us for a second and we realized they and done a dump
  and burn... started dumping fuel as they increased speed and
  pulled away from us, then hit afterburner and it ignited leaving
  a huge long fireball.
  Our ground speed was usually 8 to 10 miles per minute, so I can
  only imagine what that looked like from the ground. They
  probably dumped for 30 secs or so the fireball would have been
  maybe 5 miles long and appear moving very fast as it burned from
  back of their planes to several miles behind us... I would
  have loved to have had a video of that.
  One of the pilots asked us if we saw two UFOs streaking by?
  I also had wondered, what if we had strayed slightly off course
  into the path of the burning jp-4? What do you think it would
  have done to our tanker? They were not that far away from us
  when they did this.... maybe 1000 ft or so on each side
  Has anyone heard of any video of this type event on YouTube or
  any other site? I would love to see what it looked like from the
  ground. Or I wonder if any of you have heard of any UFO
  sightings that could be attributed to something like this prank
  being played by the world's finest airmen?
  Scott
  -----
  Terry W. Colvin
  Ladphrao (Bangkok), Thailand
  Pran Buri (Hua Hin), Thailand
  http://terrycolvin.freewebsites.com/
  [Terry's Fortean & "Work" itty-bitty site]

Tassie UFO Reports




                                                          UFO Updates
                                   A mailing list for the study of UFO-related phenomena
                                               'Its All Here In Black & White'
    Location: UFOUpDatesList.Com > 2010 > Mar > Mar 29

                                                            Tassie UFO Reports
  From: Sheryl Gottschall <gottscha.nul>
  Date: Mon, 29 Mar 2010 19:53:16 +1000
  Archived: Mon, 29 Mar 2010 08:13:44 -0400
  Subject: Tassie UFO Reports

  Source: The Mercury, Hobart, Tasmania
  March 26, 2010
  http://tinyurl.com/yar6x8j
  Tassie UFO Reports
  The chances of anything coming from Mars really are a million to
  one, says astronomer Chris Arkless.
  While Mr Arkless believes the universe is home to other life
  forms, he said human understanding of travel meant there was
  nowhere nearby for visitors to Earth to come from.
  "It would be a bold and foolish person who claimed there has
  never been or is not still life on Mars but we are talking
  bacterial forms of life, not little green men," Mr Arkless said.
  "It is impossible to travel faster than the speed of light,
  under our current understanding. Alpha Centuri, the closest star
  is 4.3 light years away so there is nowhere for aliens to come
  from to hover over Earth.
  "That does not mean there is not life elsewhere but just that
  that life could not visit here."
  Mr Arkless said a photograph received by News Limited of a UFO
  in the sky on the Lyell Highway between Queenstown and Hobart
  was a hoax which had been created through computer technology.
  The photograph was allegedly taken on Janaury 3 near the
  Franklin and Alma Rivers.
  "It does not look like an aircraft and if you zoom in you can
  see the pixels are different around the supposed UFO," he said.
  Mr Arkless said reported UFO sightings came in waves and none
  reported in Tasmania have been verified as alien craft. In
  December last year unusual lights in the sky over Hobart sparked
  a wave of concern that a plane was about to crash but a
  Tasmanian UFO hunter said it was probably a meteor or space
  junk.
  The source of the mystery lights remains unknown and Tasmania
  Police took dozens of calls from people around the state who saw
  the lights heading south.
  Ten months earlier, there were reports of strange lights over
  Launceston. The TasUFO Centre, which has investigated hundreds
  of sightings since it was established in 1965, interviewed
  people who saw the lights but did not comet to any solid
  conclusion.
  "I am a child of the space age myself and when I was a little
  kid I joined a local UFO spotting group," Mr Arkless said. "The
  sightings usually ended up being Venus.
  "Obviously there are going to be unidentified flying objects in


http://www.ufoupdateslist.com/2010/mar/m29-002.shtml[10/12/2011 14:20:08]
Tassie UFO Reports

  the sky such as unidentified aircraft, but it is highly unlikely
  any are from outer space."

                                             Australia Attracts New Kind of Visitors
  From: Sheryl Gottschall <gottscha.nul>
  Date: Tue, 30 Mar 2010 09:05:24 +1000
  Archived: Tue, 30 Mar 2010 08:53:34 -0400
  Subject: Australia Attracts New Kind of Visitors

  Source: Embrace Australia, Bath, UK
  http://tinyurl.com/yzdddwd
  29 March, 2010
  Australia Attracts New Kind Of Visitors
  Australia has always been the number one spot for migrants
  emigrating abroad, particularly British migrants, but now it
  seems that Australia is being visited by an altogether different
  kind of immigrant - and they don't have any visas!
  Quite what the Department of Immigration and Citizenship would
  make of it one can only guess, but they don't have too much to
  fear since these visitors have no intention of becoming
  immigrants to Australia, they're just here on holiday it seems.
  Yes that's right, Australia is now a popular holiday destination
  for visitors from outer space. In particular they seem to like
  Gosford in New South Wales which has become Australia's UFO
  hotspot. The Daily Telegraph reports that there are dozens of
  authentic UFO sightings each year. So popular is the town that
  UFO meetings are held every month for people to share their
  experiences of all things extra terrestrial.
  From glowing lights to spherical objects, Gosford residents have
  reported dozens of sightings and indeed the area was the scene
  of a series of puzzling sightings from 1995-1996 that remains
  unsolved to this day.
  UFO Research NSW reports that the area's geographical location
  might have something to do with it. They claim that UFOs prefer
  isolated spots near to water sources and power plants, something
  the NSW coast has plenty of. The Blue Mountains also have a high
  incidence of UFO sightings - so it seems the UFOs are just as
  impressed with Australia's magnificent scenery as the tourists
  are!
  Perhaps Tourism Australia should devise a marketing strategy
  that would appeal to the interstellar visitors - so long as they
  leave the 'Roos in Australia!
  Our alien friends might care to take a look at some of the
  Australian holiday ideas we have on offer, they probably won't
  need to find cheap flights to Australia but in case they want to
  extend their visit, we have plenty of accommodation ideas too.

                                    A mailing list for the study of UFO-related phenomena

                                            X-Conference 2010 Press Release - 3/30/10
  From: Stephen Bassett <PRG.nul>
  Date: Tue, 30 Mar 2010 03:50:56 -0700
  Archived: Tue, 30 Mar 2010 08:56:27 -0400
  Subject: X-Conference 2010 Press Release - 3/30/10

  March 30, 2010
  Press Release
  PRG
  Paradigm Research Group
  New Film Premieres at X-Conference 2010
  National Press Club May 7-9, 2010
  Washington, DC -- Paradigm Research Group (PRG) announced today
  the world premiere of a new documentary film, The Day Before
  Disclosure, will take place at X-Conference 2010 on May 7, at 9
  pm at the National Press Club (NPC) in Washington, DC. The
  conference website is at: www.x-conference.com
  The documentary is produced by New Paradigm Films and Terje
  Toftenes based in Norway. It will touch on a range of issues
  including: the growing UFO-activity in the US and around the
  world during the late 1940's and 50's; why secrecy and
  suppression became an official policy; the abduction phenomenon;
  and the new physics that may explain space travel over vast
  distances, anti-gravitic propulsion and so called "zero-point
  energy.=94
  It will also address the larger perspective and explain the
  present status of the UFO/ET issue and why we are at a point in
  human history approaching a major paradigm shift that could be
  triggered by a Disclosure event regarding an extraterrestrial
  presence engaging the human race. This and much more will be
  featured in one of the first documentaries to address the UFO/ET
  issues primarily from an exopolitical perspective. A film
  website will go up shortly.
  This is the sixth X-Conference produced by PRG, an advocacy
  organization dedicated to ending the now 62-year truth embargo
  imposed by the United States government on the formal
  acknowledgement of an extraterrestrial presence engaging the
  human race.
  Topics will include: government imposed truth embargo;
  Rockefeller Initiative; UFO/ET connections to the Obama
  administration; the Cydonia/Martian Codex; Disclosure; RAF
  Bentwaters case; Eisenhower briefing memo; UFOs and the National
  Security State; police sightings database; developments in the
  UK; extraordinary developments in Brazil; UFO/ET connections to
  Russia and the Soviet Union; and more.
  There will be 8 lectures, 2 special luncheon presentations, a
  Speaker's Cocktail Party and the PRG Awards Banquet. A press
  conference will be held on Monday, May 10 at 10 am in the NPC's
  First Amendment Room for announcements relating to the growing
  international Disclosure movement.
  The National Press Club is two blocks from the Metro Center
  Station. There are two dozen hotels close by, and PRG has
  special booking arrangements with the Harrington and J. W.
  Marriott hotels. Information on X-Conference 2010 and past
  X-Conferences is found at:

                                    Contactees: A History Of Human-Alien Interaction
  From: Sheryl Gottschall <gottscha.nul>
  Date: Wed, 31 Mar 2010 08:08:44 +1000
  Archived: Wed, 31 Mar 2010 02:24:59 -0400
  Subject: Contactees: A History Of Human-Alien Interaction

  >From: UFO UpDates - Toronto <post.nul>
  >To: - UFO UpDates Subscribers - <ufo-updates-list.nul>
  >Date: Tuesday, March 30, 2010 11:45 PM
  >Subject: UFO UpDate: News Links - 29-03-10
  <snip>
  >News Links - 29-03-10
  >Nick Redfern's Contactees: A History Of Human-Alien Interaction
  >Reviewed by Peter Rogerson
  >http://pelicanist.blogspot.com/
  I haven't read this book yet but this review mentions that Ralph
  Lael stood as a political candidate and Margit Mustapha was a
  singer.
  Over the years I've developed a profile for people who report
  close encounters and the main elements of this profile are that
  these people develop:
  1) deep social concerns and/or become political/peace/social
  activists
  2) healing abilities
  3) artistic abilities eg. paint, sculpt, sing, dance, write
  (particularly poetry) etc.
  4) environmental concerns and/or become environmental activists
  Not everyone has all elements, usually only one or two but the
  artistic element is the most reported. Although I haven't done
  any formal studies on this, in 95% of cases I've been involved
  with (hundreds) the artistic element shows up. It's extremely
  predominant. What that means I can't say for sure.
  I'd just like to mention here too that Howard Menger was an
  artist, Dan Fry wrote poetry and wrote about peace, Elizabeth
  Klarer was an artist and an accomplished pianist who performed
  at concert level and Betty & Barney Hill were social activists.
  Remember the Allagash case? The four witnesses had an artistic
  background... and the list goes on.
  Something to think about.

  Sheryl

  Well that adds meaning to the old legend of being kissed by a
  Muse.
  I know a secret! I know a secret!
  Sheryl, I think you're so on target there's no need for anyone


http://www.ufoupdateslist.com/2010/mar/m31-003.shtml[10/12/2011 14:20:12]
Re: Contactees: A History Of Human-Alien

  else to step up to the shooting range.
  What opens the doors to creativity or what removes the blocks we
  allow to be set up apparently get removed during these
  encounters. That says a lot about the functions of the various
  minds we possess. Further analysis of the data that researchers
  like you have explored needs to be done and I would say as a
  priority by mankind in general.
  However, being a professional artist, writer, I've seen from
  experience how having an imagination can get you targeted by
  those afraid of anyone with any ounce of creativity. We've all
  probably become familiar with the suppressed artist. From Mozart
  on up it's a common occurrence. What is it that artists do that
  leads them into these situations is another big question.
  Working in comics and animation and fine art, it's a common
  thing to find artists who shy away from their administrative
  duties as in business and business strategies. We're preyed upon
  by those who cannot create, don't have imagination on an
  aesthetic level but sure have a barn full on how to exploit the
  talents of others.
  If in these experiences a person's creative power increases
  that's a good thing. If their awareness and concern about their
  fellow man, life forms, etc. increases that's a good thing too.
  However, I've seen some folks turned insane from these same
  experiences. The communication procedures by our visitors is on
  a different level and the person interprets these communications
  by whatever education and awareness and cultural means. That can
  spell disaster. Stress, high blood pressure, headaches, and any
  number of psychosomatic illnesses and manifestations.
  Some survive the encounters and grow, some get so restimulated
  on a negative level that they collapse.
  We need more research and what you and others are doing in my
  opinion is so cutting edge, so many light years ahead that
  the rewards will be astronomical, literally.

  Best,
  Greg
